```
    87→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠈⠳⠈⣡⠞⠁⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]

    90→COMPACT_BANNER = """
    91→[bold #FFD700]╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗[/]
    92→[bold #FFD700]║[/]  [#FFBF00]⚕ NOUS HERMES[/] [dim #B8860B]- AI Agent Framework[/]              [bold #FFD700]║[/]
    93→[bold #FFD700]║[/]  [#CD7F32]Messenger of the Digital Gods[/]    [dim #B8860B]Nous Research[/]   [bold #FFD700]║[/]
    94→[bold #FFD700]╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝[/]
    95→"""
    96→
    97→
    98→# =========================================================================
    99→# Skills scanning
   100→# =========================================================================
   101→
   102→def get_available_skills() -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
   103→    """Scan ~/.hermes/skills/ and return skills grouped by category."""
   104→    import os
   105→
   106→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   107→    skills_dir = hermes_home / "skills"
   108→    skills_by_category = {}
   109→
   110→    if not skills_dir.exists():
   111→        return skills_by_category
   112→
   113→    for skill_file in skills_dir.rglob("SKILL.md"):
   114→        rel_path = skill_file.relative_to(skills_dir)
   115→        parts = rel_path.parts
   116→        if len(parts) >= 2:
   117→            category = parts[0]
   118→            skill_name = parts[-2]
   119→        else:
   120→            category = "general"
   121→            skill_name = skill_file.parent.name
   122→        skills_by_category.setdefault(category, []).append(skill_name)
   123→
   124→    return skills_by_category
   125→
   126→
   127→# =========================================================================
   128→# Update check
   129→# =========================================================================
   130→
   131→# Cache update check results for 6 hours to avoid repeated git fetches
   132→_UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS = 6 * 3600
   133→
   134→
   135→def check_for_updates() -> Optional[int]:
   136→    """Check how many commits behind origin/main the local repo is.
   137→
   138→    Does a ``git fetch`` at most once every 6 hours (cached to
   139→    ``~/.hermes/.update_check``).  Returns the number of commits behind,
   140→    or ``None`` if the check fails or isn't applicable.
   141→    """
   142→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   143→    repo_dir = hermes_home / "hermes-agent"
   144→    cache_file = hermes_home / ".update_check"
   145→
   146→    # Must be a git repo
   147→    if not (repo_dir / ".git").exists():
   148→        return None
   149→
   150→    # Read cache
   151→    now = time.time()
   152→    try:
   153→        if cache_file.exists():
   154→            cached = json.loads(cache_file.read_text())
   155→            if now - cached.get("ts", 0) < _UPDATE_CHECK_CACHE_SECONDS:
   156→                return cached.get("behind")
   157→    except Exception:
   158→        pass
   159→
   160→    # Fetch latest refs (fast — only downloads ref metadata, no files)
   161→    try:
   162→        subprocess.run(
   163→            ["git", "fetch", "origin", "--quiet"],
   164→            capture_output=True, timeout=10,
   165→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   166→        )
   167→    except Exception:
   168→        pass  # Offline or timeout — use stale refs, that's fine
   169→
   170→    # Count commits behind
   171→    try:
   172→        result = subprocess.run(
   173→            ["git", "rev-list", "--count", "HEAD..origin/main"],
   174→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5,
   175→            cwd=str(repo_dir),
   176→        )
   177→        if result.returncode == 0:
   178→            behind = int(result.stdout.strip())
   179→        else:
   180→            behind = None
   181→    except Exception:
   182→        behind = None
   183→
   184→    # Write cache
   185→    try:
   186→        cache_file.write_text(json.dumps({"ts": now, "behind": behind}))
   187→    except Exception:
   188→        pass
   189→
   190→    return behind
   191→
   192→
   193→# =========================================================================
   194→# Welcome banner
   195→# =========================================================================
   196→
   197→def _format_context_length(tokens: int) -> str:
   198→    """Format a token count for display (e.g. 128000 → '128K', 1048576 → '1M')."""
   199→    if tokens >= 1_000_000:
   200→        val = tokens / 1_000_000
   201→        return f"{val:g}M"
   202→    elif tokens >= 1_000:
   203→        val = tokens / 1_000
   204→        return f"{val:g}K"
   205→    return str(tokens)
   206→
   207→
   208→def build_welcome_banner(console: Console, model: str, cwd: str,
   209→                         tools: List[dict] = None,
   210→                         enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   211→                         session_id: str = None,
   212→                         get_toolset_for_tool=None,
   213→                         context_length: int = None):
   214→    """Build and print a welcome banner with caduceus on left and info on right.
   215→
   216→    Args:
   217→        console: Rich Console instance.
   218→        model: Current model name.
   219→        cwd: Current working directory.
   220→        tools: List of tool definitions.
   221→        enabled_toolsets: List of enabled toolset names.
   222→        session_id: Session identifier.
   223→        get_toolset_for_tool: Callable to map tool name -> toolset name.
   224→        context_length: Model's context window size in tokens.
   225→    """
   226→    from model_tools import check_tool_availability, TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS
   227→    if get_toolset_for_tool is None:
   228→        from model_tools import get_toolset_for_tool
   229→
   230→    tools = tools or []
   231→    enabled_toolsets = enabled_toolsets or []
   232→
   233→    _, unavailable_toolsets = check_tool_availability(quiet=True)
   234→    disabled_tools = set()
   235→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   236→        disabled_tools.update(item.get("tools", []))
   237→
   238→    layout_table = Table.grid(padding=(0, 2))
   239→    layout_table.add_column("left", justify="center")
   240→    layout_table.add_column("right", justify="left")
   241→
   242→    # Resolve skin colors once for the entire banner
   243→    accent = _skin_color("banner_accent", "#FFBF00")
   244→    dim = _skin_color("banner_dim", "#B8860B")
   245→    text = _skin_color("banner_text", "#FFF8DC")
   246→    session_color = _skin_color("session_border", "#8B8682")
   247→
   248→    left_lines = ["", HERMES_CADUCEUS, ""]
   249→    model_short = model.split("/")[-1] if "/" in model else model
   250→    if len(model_short) > 28:
   251→        model_short = model_short[:25] + "..."
   252→    ctx_str = f" [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]{_format_context_length(context_length)} context[/]" if context_length else ""
   253→    left_lines.append(f"[{accent}]{model_short}[/]{ctx_str} [dim {dim}]·[/] [dim {dim}]Nous Research[/]")
   254→    left_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{cwd}[/]")
   255→    if session_id:
   256→        left_lines.append(f"[dim {session_color}]Session: {session_id}[/]")
   257→    left_content = "\n".join(left_lines)
   258→
   259→    right_lines = [f"[bold {accent}]Available Tools[/]"]
   260→    toolsets_dict: Dict[str, list] = {}
   261→
   262→    for tool in tools:
   263→        tool_name = tool["function"]["name"]
   264→        toolset = get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name) or "other"
   265→        toolsets_dict.setdefault(toolset, []).append(tool_name)
   266→
   267→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   268→        toolset_id = item.get("id", item.get("name", "unknown"))
   269→        display_name = f"{toolset_id}_tools" if not toolset_id.endswith("_tools") else toolset_id
   270→        if display_name not in toolsets_dict:
   271→            toolsets_dict[display_name] = []
   272→        for tool_name in item.get("tools", []):
   273→            if tool_name not in toolsets_dict[display_name]:
   274→                toolsets_dict[display_name].append(tool_name)
   275→
   276→    sorted_toolsets = sorted(toolsets_dict.keys())
   277→    display_toolsets = sorted_toolsets[:8]
   278→    remaining_toolsets = len(sorted_toolsets) - 8
   279→
   280→    for toolset in display_toolsets:
   281→        tool_names = toolsets_dict[toolset]
   282→        colored_names = []
   283→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
   284→            if name in disabled_tools:
   285→                colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   286→            else:
   287→                colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   288→
   289→        tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   290→        if len(", ".join(sorted(tool_names))) > 45:
   291→            short_names = []
   292→            length = 0
   293→            for name in sorted(tool_names):
   294→                if length + len(name) + 2 > 42:
   295→                    short_names.append("...")
   296→                    break
   297→                short_names.append(name)
   298→                length += len(name) + 2
   299→            colored_names = []
   300→            for name in short_names:
   301→                if name == "...":
   302→                    colored_names.append("[dim]...[/]")
   303→                elif name in disabled_tools:
   304→                    colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   305→                else:
   306→                    colored_names.append(f"[{text}]{name}[/]")
   307→            tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   308→
   309→        right_lines.append(f"[dim #B8860B]{toolset}:[/] {tools_str}")
   310→
   311→    if remaining_toolsets > 0:
   312→        right_lines.append(f"[dim #B8860B](and {remaining_toolsets} more toolsets...)[/]")
   313→
   314→    # MCP Servers section (only if configured)
   315→    try:
   316→        from tools.mcp_tool import get_mcp_status
   317→        mcp_status = get_mcp_status()
   318→    except Exception:
   319→        mcp_status = []
   320→
   321→    if mcp_status:
   322→        right_lines.append("")
   323→        right_lines.append("[bold #FFBF00]MCP Servers[/]")
   324→        for srv in mcp_status:
   325→            if srv["connected"]:
   326→                right_lines.append(
   327→                    f"[dim #B8860B]{srv['name']}[/] [#FFF8DC]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   328→                    f"[dim #B8860B]—[/] [#FFF8DC]{srv['tools']} tool(s)[/]"
   329→                )
   330→            else:
   331→                right_lines.append(
   332→                    f"[red]{srv['name']}[/] [dim]({srv['transport']})[/] "
   333→                    f"[red]— failed[/]"
   334→                )
   335→
   336→    right_lines.append("")
   337→    right_lines.append(f"[bold {accent}]Available Skills[/]")
   338→    skills_by_category = get_available_skills()
   339→    total_skills = sum(len(s) for s in skills_by_category.values())
   340→
   341→    if skills_by_category:
   342→        for category in sorted(skills_by_category.keys()):
   343→            skill_names = sorted(skills_by_category[category])
   344→            if len(skill_names) > 8:
   345→                display_names = skill_names[:8]
   346→                skills_str = ", ".join(display_names) + f" +{len(skill_names) - 8} more"
   347→            else:
   348→                skills_str = ", ".join(skill_names)
   349→            if len(skills_str) > 50:
   350→                skills_str = skills_str[:47] + "..."
   351→            right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{category}:[/] [{text}]{skills_str}[/]")
   352→    else:
   353→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]No skills installed[/]")
   354→
   355→    right_lines.append("")
   356→    mcp_connected = sum(1 for s in mcp_status if s["connected"]) if mcp_status else 0
   357→    summary_parts = [f"{len(tools)} tools", f"{total_skills} skills"]
   358→    if mcp_connected:
   359→        summary_parts.append(f"{mcp_connected} MCP servers")
   360→    summary_parts.append("/help for commands")
   361→    right_lines.append(f"[dim {dim}]{' · '.join(summary_parts)}[/]")
   362→
   363→    # Update check — show if behind origin/main
   364→    try:
   365→        behind = check_for_updates()
   366→        if behind and behind > 0:
   367→            commits_word = "commit" if behind == 1 else "commits"
   368→            right_lines.append(
   369→                f"[bold yellow]⚠ {behind} {commits_word} behind[/]"
   370→                f"[dim yellow] — run [bold]hermes update[/bold] to update[/]"
   371→            )
   372→    except Exception:
   373→        pass  # Never break the banner over an update check
   374→
   375→    right_content = "\n".join(right_lines)
   376→    layout_table.add_row(left_content, right_content)
   377→
   378→    agent_name = _skin_branding("agent_name", "Hermes Agent")
   379→    title_color = _skin_color("banner_title", "#FFD700")
   380→    border_color = _skin_color("banner_border", "#CD7F32")
   381→    outer_panel = Panel(
   382→        layout_table,
   383→        title=f"[bold {title_color}]{agent_name} v{VERSION} ({RELEASE_DATE})[/]",
   384→        border_style=border_color,
   385→        padding=(0, 2),
   386→    )
   387→
   388→    console.print()
   389→    console.print(HERMES_AGENT_LOGO)
   390→    console.print()
   391→    console.print(outer_panel)
   392→
```

```
1→"""Interactive prompt callbacks for terminal_tool integration.
     2→
     3→These bridge terminal_tool's interactive prompts (clarify, sudo, approval)
     4→into prompt_toolkit's event loop. Each function takes the HermesCLI instance
     5→as its first argument and uses its state (queues, app reference) to coordinate
     6→with the TUI.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import queue
    10→import time as _time
    11→import getpass
    12→
    13→from hermes_cli.banner import cprint, _DIM, _RST
    14→from hermes_cli.config import save_env_value_secure
    15→
    16→
    17→def clarify_callback(cli, question, choices):
    18→    """Prompt for clarifying question through the TUI.
    19→
    20→    Sets up the interactive selection UI, then blocks until the user
    21→    responds. Returns the user's choice or a timeout message.
    22→    """
    23→    from cli import CLI_CONFIG
    24→
    25→    timeout = CLI_CONFIG.get("clarify", {}).get("timeout", 120)
    26→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
    27→    is_open_ended = not choices or len(choices) == 0
    28→
    29→    cli._clarify_state = {
    30→        "question": question,
    31→        "choices": choices if not is_open_ended else [],
    32→        "selected": 0,
    33→        "response_queue": response_queue,
    34→    }
    35→    cli._clarify_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
    36→    cli._clarify_freetext = is_open_ended
    37→
    38→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    39→        cli._app.invalidate()
    40→
    41→    while True:
    42→        try:
    43→            result = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
    44→            cli._clarify_deadline = 0
    45→            return result
    46→        except queue.Empty:
    47→            remaining = cli._clarify_deadline - _time.monotonic()
    48→            if remaining <= 0:
    49→                break
    50→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    51→                cli._app.invalidate()
    52→
    53→    cli._clarify_state = None
    54→    cli._clarify_freetext = False
    55→    cli._clarify_deadline = 0
    56→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    57→        cli._app.invalidate()
    58→    cprint(f"\n{_DIM}(clarify timed out after {timeout}s — agent will decide){_RST}")
    59→    return (
    60→        "The user did not provide a response within the time limit. "
    61→        "Use your best judgement to make the choice and proceed."
    62→    )
    63→
    64→
    65→def sudo_[REDACTED](cli) -> str:
    66→    """Prompt for sudo password through the TUI.
    67→
    68→    Sets up a password input area and blocks until the user responds.
    69→    """
    70→    timeout = 45
    71→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
    72→
    73→    cli._sudo_state = {"response_queue": response_queue}
    74→    cli._sudo_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
    75→
    76→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    77→        cli._app.invalidate()
    78→
    79→    while True:
    80→        try:
    81→            result = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
    82→            cli._sudo_state = None
    83→            cli._sudo_deadline = 0
    84→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    85→                cli._app.invalidate()
    86→            if result:
    87→                cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ✓ [REDACTED] (cached for session){_RST}")
    88→            else:
    89→                cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏭ Skipped{_RST}")
    90→            return result
    91→        except queue.Empty:
    92→            remaining = cli._sudo_deadline - _time.monotonic()
    93→            if remaining <= 0:
    94→                break
    95→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    96→                cli._app.invalidate()
    97→
    98→    cli._sudo_state = None
    99→    cli._sudo_deadline = 0
   100→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   101→        cli._app.invalidate()
   102→    cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏱ Timeout — continuing without sudo{_RST}")
   103→    return ""
   104→
   105→
   106→def prompt_for_secret(cli, var_name: str, prompt: str, metadata=None) -> dict:
   107→    """Prompt for a secret value through the TUI (e.g. API keys for skills).
   108→
   109→    Returns a dict with keys: success, stored_as, validated, skipped, message.
   110→    The secret is stored in ~/.hermes/.env and never exposed to the model.
   111→    """
   112→    if not getattr(cli, "_app", None):
   113→        if not hasattr(cli, "_secret_state"):
   114→            cli._secret_state = None
   115→        if not hasattr(cli, "_secret_deadline"):
   116→            cli._secret_deadline = 0
   117→        try:
   118→            value = getpass.getpass(f"{prompt} (hidden, Enter to skip): ")
   119→        except (EOFError, KeyboardInterrupt):
   120→            value = ""
   121→
   122→        if not value:
   123→            cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏭ Secret entry cancelled{_RST}")
   124→            return {
   125→                "success": True,
   126→                "reason": "cancelled",
   127→                "stored_as": var_name,
   128→                "validated": False,
   129→                "skipped": True,
   130→                "message": "Secret setup was skipped.",
   131→            }
   132→
   133→        stored = save_env_value_secure(var_name, value)
   134→        cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ✓ Stored secret in ~/.hermes/.env as {var_name}{_RST}")
   135→        return {
   136→            **stored,
   137→            "skipped": False,
   138→            "message": "Secret stored securely. The secret value was not exposed to the model.",
   139→        }
   140→
   141→    timeout = 120
   142→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
   143→
   144→    cli._secret_state = {
   145→        "var_name": var_name,
   146→        "prompt": prompt,
   147→        "metadata": metadata or {},
   148→        "response_queue": response_queue,
   149→    }
   150→    cli._secret_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
   151→    # Avoid storing stale draft input as the secret when Enter is pressed.
   152→    if hasattr(cli, "_clear_secret_input_buffer"):
   153→        try:
   154→            cli._clear_secret_input_buffer()
   155→        except Exception:
   156→            pass
   157→    elif hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   158→        try:
   159→            cli._app.current_buffer.reset()
   160→        except Exception:
   161→            pass
   162→
   163→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   164→        cli._app.invalidate()
   165→
   166→    while True:
   167→        try:
   168→            value = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
   169→            cli._secret_state = None
   170→            cli._secret_deadline = 0
   171→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   172→                cli._app.invalidate()
   173→
   174→            if not value:
   175→                cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏭ Secret entry cancelled{_RST}")
   176→                return {
   177→                    "success": True,
   178→                    "reason": "cancelled",
   179→                    "stored_as": var_name,
   180→                    "validated": False,
   181→                    "skipped": True,
   182→                    "message": "Secret setup was skipped.",
   183→                }
   184→
   185→            stored = save_env_value_secure(var_name, value)
   186→            cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ✓ Stored secret in ~/.hermes/.env as {var_name}{_RST}")
   187→            return {
   188→                **stored,
   189→                "skipped": False,
   190→                "message": "Secret stored securely. The secret value was not exposed to the model.",
   191→            }
   192→        except queue.Empty:
   193→            remaining = cli._secret_deadline - _time.monotonic()
   194→            if remaining <= 0:
   195→                break
   196→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   197→                cli._app.invalidate()
   198→
   199→    cli._secret_state = None
   200→    cli._secret_deadline = 0
   201→    if hasattr(cli, "_clear_secret_input_buffer"):
   202→        try:
   203→            cli._clear_secret_input_buffer()
   204→        except Exception:
   205→            pass
   206→    elif hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   207→        try:
   208→            cli._app.current_buffer.reset()
   209→        except Exception:
   210→            pass
   211→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   212→        cli._app.invalidate()
   213→    cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏱ Timeout — secret capture cancelled{_RST}")
   214→    return {
   215→        "success": True,
   216→        "reason": "timeout",
   217→        "stored_as": var_name,
   218→        "validated": False,
   219→        "skipped": True,
   220→        "message": "Secret setup timed out and was skipped.",
   221→    }
   222→
   223→
   224→def approval_callback(cli, command: str, description: str) -> str:
   225→    """Prompt for dangerous command approval through the TUI.
   226→
   227→    Shows a selection UI with choices: once / session / always / deny.
   228→    When the command is longer than 70 characters, a "view" option is
   229→    included so the user can reveal the full text before deciding.
   230→    """
   231→    timeout = 60
   232→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
   233→    choices = ["once", "session", "always", "deny"]
   234→    if len(command) > 70:
   235→        choices.append("view")
   236→
   237→    cli._approval_state = {
   238→        "command": command,
   239→        "description": description,
   240→        "choices": choices,
   241→        "selected": 0,
   242→        "response_queue": response_queue,
   243→    }
   244→    cli._approval_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
   245→
   246→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   247→        cli._app.invalidate()
   248→
   249→    while True:
   250→        try:
   251→            result = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
   252→            cli._approval_state = None
   253→            cli._approval_deadline = 0
   254→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   255→                cli._app.invalidate()
   256→            return result
   257→        except queue.Empty:
   258→            remaining = cli._approval_deadline - _time.monotonic()
   259→            if remaining <= 0:
   260→                break
   261→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   262→                cli._app.invalidate()
   263→
   264→    cli._approval_state = None
   265→    cli._approval_deadline = 0
   266→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
   267→        cli._app.invalidate()
   268→    cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏱ Timeout — denying command{_RST}")
   269→    return "deny"
   270→
```

```
   172→# =========================================================================
   173→# KawaiiSpinner
   174→# =========================================================================
   175→
   176→class KawaiiSpinner:
   177→    """Animated spinner with kawaii faces for CLI feedback during tool execution."""
   178→
   179→    SPINNERS = {
   180→        'dots': ['⠋', '⠙', '⠹', '⠸', '⠼', '⠴', '⠦', '⠧', '⠇', '⠏'],
   181→        'bounce': ['⠁', '⠂', '⠄', '⡀', '⢀', '⠠', '⠐', '⠈'],
   182→        'grow': ['▁', '▂', '▃', '▄', '▅', '▆', '▇', '█', '▇', '▆', '▅', '▄', '▃', '▂'],
   183→        'arrows': ['←', '↖', '↑', '↗', '→', '↘', '↓', '↙'],
   184→        'star': ['✶', '✷', '✸', '✹', '✺', '✹', '✸', '✷'],
   185→        'moon': ['🌑', '🌒', '🌓', '🌔', '🌕', '🌖', '🌗', '🌘'],
   186→        'pulse': ['◜', '◠', '◝', '◞', '◡', '◟'],
   187→        'brain': ['🧠', '💭', '💡', '✨', '💫', '🌟', '💡', '💭'],
   188→        'sparkle': ['⁺', '˚', '*', '✧', '✦', '✧', '*', '˚'],
   189→    }
   190→
   191→    KAWAII_WAITING = [
   192→        "(｡◕‿◕｡)", "(◕‿◕✿)", "٩(◕‿◕｡)۶", "(✿◠‿◠)", "( ˘▽˘)っ",
   193→        "♪(´ε` )", "(◕ᴗ◕✿)", "ヾ(＾∇＾)", "(≧◡≦)", "(★ω★)",
   194→    ]
   195→
   196→    KAWAII_THINKING = [
   197→        "(｡•́︿•̀｡)", "(◔_◔)", "(¬‿¬)", "( •_•)>⌐■-■", "(⌐■_■)",
   198→        "(´･_･`)", "◉_◉", "(°ロ°)", "( ˘⌣˘)♡", "ヽ(>∀<☆)☆",
   199→        "٩(๑❛ᴗ❛๑)۶", "(⊙_⊙)", "(¬_¬)", "( ͡° ͜ʖ ͡°)", "ಠ_ಠ",
   200→    ]
   201→
   202→    THINKING_VERBS = [
   203→        "pondering", "contemplating", "musing", "cogitating", "ruminating",
   204→        "deliberating", "mulling", "reflecting", "processing", "reasoning",
   205→        "analyzing", "computing", "synthesizing", "formulating", "brainstorming",
   206→    ]
   207→
   208→    def __init__(self, message: str = "", spinner_type: str = 'dots'):
   209→        self.message = message
   210→        self.spinner_frames = self.SPINNERS.get(spinner_type, self.SPINNERS['dots'])
   211→        self.running = False
   212→        self.thread = None
   213→        self.frame_idx = 0
   214→        self.start_time = None
   215→        self.last_line_len = 0
   216→        self._last_flush_time = 0.0  # Rate-limit flushes for patch_stdout compat
   217→        # Capture stdout NOW, before any redirect_stdout(devnull) from
   218→        # child agents can replace sys.stdout with a black hole.
   219→        self._out = sys.stdout
   220→
   221→    def _write(self, text: str, end: str = '\n', flush: bool = False):
   222→        """Write to the stdout captured at spinner creation time."""
   223→        try:
   224→            self._out.write(text + end)
   225→            if flush:
   226→                self._out.flush()
   227→        except (ValueError, OSError):
   228→            pass
   229→
   230→    def _animate(self):
   231→        # Cache skin wings at start (avoid per-frame imports)
   232→        skin = _get_skin()
   233→        wings = skin.get_spinner_wings() if skin else []
   234→
   235→        while self.running:
   236→            if os.getenv("HERMES_SPINNER_PAUSE"):
   237→                time.sleep(0.1)
   238→                continue
   239→            frame = self.spinner_frames[self.frame_idx % len(self.spinner_frames)]
   240→            elapsed = time.time() - self.start_time
   241→            if wings:
   242→                left, right = wings[self.frame_idx % len(wings)]
   243→                line = f"  {left} {frame} {self.message} {right} ({elapsed:.1f}s)"
   244→            else:
   245→                line = f"  {frame} {self.message} ({elapsed:.1f}s)"
   246→            pad = max(self.last_line_len - len(line), 0)
   247→            # Rate-limit flush() calls to avoid spinner spam under
   248→            # prompt_toolkit's patch_stdout.  Each flush() pushes a queue
   249→            # item that may trigger a separate run_in_terminal() call; if
   250→            # items are processed one-at-a-time the \r overwrite is lost
   251→            # and every frame appears on its own line.  By flushing at
   252→            # most every 0.4s we guarantee multiple \r-frames are batched
   253→            # into a single write, so the terminal collapses them correctly.
   254→            now = time.time()
   255→            should_flush = (now - self._last_flush_time) >= 0.4
   256→            self._write(f"\r{line}{' ' * pad}", end='', flush=should_flush)
   257→            if should_flush:
   258→                self._last_flush_time = now
   259→            self.last_line_len = len(line)
   260→            self.frame_idx += 1
   261→            time.sleep(0.12)
   262→
   263→    def start(self):
   264→        if self.running:
   265→            return
   266→        self.running = True
   267→        self.start_time = time.time()
   268→        self.thread = threading.Thread(target=self._animate, daemon=True)
   269→        self.thread.start()
   270→
   271→    def update_text(self, new_message: str):
   272→        self.message = new_message
   273→
   274→    def print_above(self, text: str):
   275→        """Print a line above the spinner without disrupting animation.
   276→
   277→        Clears the current spinner line, prints the text, and lets the
   278→        next animation tick redraw the spinner on the line below.
   279→        Thread-safe: uses the captured stdout reference (self._out).
   280→        Works inside redirect_stdout(devnull) because _write bypasses
   281→        sys.stdout and writes to the stdout captured at spinner creation.
   282→        """
   283→        if not self.running:
   284→            self._write(f"  {text}", flush=True)
   285→            return
   286→        # Clear spinner line with spaces (not \033[K) to avoid garbled escape
   287→        # codes when prompt_toolkit's patch_stdout is active — same approach
   288→        # as stop(). Then print text; spinner redraws on next tick.
   289→        blanks = ' ' * max(self.last_line_len + 5, 40)
   290→        self._write(f"\r{blanks}\r  {text}", flush=True)
   291→
   292→    def stop(self, final_message: str = None):
   293→        self.running = False
   294→        if self.thread:
   295→            self.thread.join(timeout=0.5)
   296→        # Clear the spinner line with spaces instead of \033[K to avoid
   297→        # garbled escape codes when prompt_toolkit's patch_stdout is active.
   298→        blanks = ' ' * max(self.last_line_len + 5, 40)
   299→        self._write(f"\r{blanks}\r", end='', flush=True)
   300→        if final_message:
   301→            self._write(f"  {final_message}", flush=True)
   302→
   303→    def __enter__(self):
   304→        self.start()
   305→        return self
   306→
   307→    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc_val, exc_tb):
   308→        self.stop()
   309→        return False

   356→def _detect_tool_failure(tool_name: str, result: str | None) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   357→    """Inspect a tool result string for signs of failure.
   358→
   359→    Returns ``(is_failure, suffix)`` where *suffix* is an informational tag
   360→    like ``" [exit 1]"`` for terminal failures, or ``" [error]"`` for generic
   361→    failures.  On success, returns ``(False, "")``.
   362→    """
   363→    if result is None:
   364→        return False, ""
   365→
   366→    if tool_name == "terminal":
   367→        try:
   368→            data = json.loads(result)
   369→            exit_code = data.get("exit_code")
   370→            if exit_code is not None and exit_code != 0:
   371→                return True, f" [exit {exit_code}]"
   372→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError, AttributeError):
   373→            logger.debug("Could not parse terminal result as JSON for exit code check")
   374→        return False, ""
   375→
   376→    # Memory-specific: distinguish "full" from real errors
   377→    if tool_name == "memory":
   378→        try:
   379→            data = json.loads(result)
   380→            if data.get("success") is False and "exceed the limit" in data.get("error", ""):
   381→                return True, " [full]"
   382→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError, AttributeError):
   383→            logger.debug("Could not parse memory result as JSON for capacity check")
   384→
   385→    # Generic heuristic for non-terminal tools
   386→    lower = result[:500].lower()
   387→    if '"error"' in lower or '"failed"' in lower or result.startswith("Error"):
   388→        return True, " [error]"
   389→
   390→    return False, ""
   391→
   392→
   393→def get_cute_tool_message(
   394→    tool_name: str, args: dict, duration: float, result: str | None = None,
   395→) -> str:
   396→    """Generate a formatted tool completion line for CLI quiet mode.
   397→
   398→    Format: ``| {emoji} {verb:9} {detail}  {duration}``
   399→
   400→    When *result* is provided the line is checked for failure indicators.
   401→    Failed tool calls get a red prefix and an informational suffix.
   402→    """
   403→    dur = f"{duration:.1f}s"
   404→    is_failure, failure_suffix = _detect_tool_failure(tool_name, result)
   405→    skin_prefix = get_skin_tool_prefix()
   406→
   407→    def _trunc(s, n=40):
   408→        s = str(s)
   409→        return (s[:n-3] + "...") if len(s) > n else s
   410→
   411→    def _path(p, n=35):
   412→        p = str(p)
   413→        return ("..." + p[-(n-3):]) if len(p) > n else p
   414→
   415→    def _wrap(line: str) -> str:
   416→        """Apply skin tool prefix and failure suffix."""
   417→        if skin_prefix != "┊":
   418→            line = line.replace("┊", skin_prefix, 1)
   419→        if not is_failure:
   420→            return line
   421→        return f"{line}{failure_suffix}"
   422→
   423→    if tool_name == "web_search":
   424→        return _wrap(f"┊ 🔍 search    {_trunc(args.get('query', ''), 42)}  {dur}")
   425→    if tool_name == "web_extract":
   426→        urls = args.get("urls", [])
   427→        if urls:
   428→            url = urls[0] if isinstance(urls, list) else str(urls)
   429→            domain = url.replace("https://", "").replace("http://", "").split("/")[0]
   430→            extra = f" +{len(urls)-1}" if len(urls) > 1 else ""
   431→            return _wrap(f"┊ 📄 fetch     {_trunc(domain, 35)}{extra}  {dur}")
   432→        return _wrap(f"┊ 📄 fetch     pages  {dur}")
   433→    if tool_name == "web_crawl":
   434→        url = args.get("url", "")
   435→        domain = url.replace("https://", "").replace("http://", "").split("/")[0]
   436→        return _wrap(f"┊ 🕸️  crawl     {_trunc(domain, 35)}  {dur}")
   437→    if tool_name == "terminal":
   438→        return _wrap(f"┊ 💻 $         {_trunc(args.get('command', ''), 42)}  {dur}")
   439→    if tool_name == "process":
   440→        action = args.get("action", "?")
   441→        sid = args.get("session_id", "")[:12]
   442→        labels = {"list": "ls processes", "poll": f"poll {sid}", "log": f"log {sid}",
   443→                  "wait": f"wait {sid}", "kill": f"kill {sid}", "write": f"write {sid}", "submit": f"submit {sid}"}
   444→        return _wrap(f"┊ ⚙️  proc      {labels.get(action, f'{action} {sid}')}  {dur}")
   445→    if tool_name == "read_file":
   446→        return _wrap(f"┊ 📖 read      {_path(args.get('path', ''))}  {dur}")
   447→    if tool_name == "write_file":
   448→        return _wrap(f"┊ ✍️  write     {_path(args.get('path', ''))}  {dur}")
   449→    if tool_name == "patch":
   450→        return _wrap(f"┊ 🔧 patch     {_path(args.get('path', ''))}  {dur}")
   451→    if tool_name == "search_files":
   452→        pattern = _trunc(args.get("pattern", ""), 35)
   453→        target = args.get("target", "content")
   454→        verb = "find" if target == "files" else "grep"
   455→        return _wrap(f"┊ 🔎 {verb:9} {pattern}  {dur}")
   456→    if tool_name == "browser_navigate":
   457→        url = args.get("url", "")
   458→        domain = url.replace("https://", "").replace("http://", "").split("/")[0]
   459→        return _wrap(f"┊ 🌐 navigate  {_trunc(domain, 35)}  {dur}")
   460→    if tool_name == "browser_snapshot":
   461→        mode = "full" if args.get("full") else "compact"

   463→    if tool_name == "browser_click":

   465→    if tool_name == "browser_type":
   466→        return _wrap(f"┊ ⌨️  type      \"{_trunc(args.get('text', ''), 30)}\"  {dur}")
   467→    if tool_name == "browser_scroll":
   468→        d = args.get("direction", "down")
   469→        arrow = {"down": "↓", "up": "↑", "right": "→", "left": "←"}.get(d, "↓")
   470→        return _wrap(f"┊ {arrow}  scroll    {d}  {dur}")
   471→    if tool_name == "browser_back":

   545→# =========================================================================
   546→# Honcho session line (one-liner with clickable OSC 8 hyperlink)
   547→# =========================================================================
   548→
   549→_DIM = "\033[2m"
   550→_SKY_BLUE = "\033[38;5;117m"
   551→_ANSI_RESET = "\033[0m"
   552→
   553→
   554→def honcho_session_url(workspace: str, session_name: str) -> str:
   555→    """Build a Honcho app URL for a session."""
   556→    from urllib.parse import quote
   557→    return (
   558→        f"https://app.honcho.dev/explore"
   559→        f"?workspace={quote(workspace, safe='')}"
   560→        f"&view=sessions"
   561→        f"&session={quote(session_name, safe='')}"
   562→    )
   563→
   564→
   565→def _osc8_link(url: str, text: str) -> str:
   566→    """OSC 8 terminal hyperlink (clickable in iTerm2, Ghostty, WezTerm, etc.)."""
   567→    return f"\033]8;;{url}\033\\{text}\033]8;;\033\\"
   568→
   569→
   570→def honcho_session_line(workspace: str, session_name: str) -> str:
   571→    """One-line session indicator: `Honcho session: <clickable name>`."""
   572→    url = honcho_session_url(workspace, session_name)
   573→    linked_name = _osc8_link(url, f"{_SKY_BLUE}{session_name}{_ANSI_RESET}")
   574→    return f"{_DIM}Honcho session:{_ANSI_RESET} {linked_name}"
   575→
   576→
   577→def write_tty(text: str) -> None:
   578→    """Write directly to /dev/tty, bypassing stdout capture."""
   579→    try:
   580→        fd = os.open("/dev/tty", os.O_WRONLY)
   581→        os.write(fd, text.encode("utf-8"))
   582→        os.close(fd)
   583→    except OSError:
   584→        sys.stdout.write(text)
   585→        sys.stdout.flush()
   586→
```

```
     4→Modular wizard with independently-runnable sections:

    26→def _model_config_dict(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    27→    current_model = config.get("model")
    28→    if isinstance(current_model, dict):
    29→        return dict(current_model)
    30→    if isinstance(current_model, str) and current_model.strip():
    31→        return {"default": current_model.strip()}
    32→    return {}
    33→
    34→
    35→def _set_model_provider(
    36→    config: Dict[str, Any], provider_id: str, base_url: str = ""
    37→) -> None:
    38→    model_cfg = _model_config_dict(config)
    39→    model_cfg["provider"] = provider_id
    40→    if base_url:
    41→        model_cfg["base_url"] = base_url.rstrip("/")
    42→    else:
    43→        model_cfg.pop("base_url", None)
    44→    config["model"] = model_cfg
    45→
    46→
    47→def _set_default_model(config: Dict[str, Any], model_name: str) -> None:
    48→    if not model_name:
    49→        return
    50→    model_cfg = _model_config_dict(config)
    51→    model_cfg["default"] = model_name
    52→    config["model"] = model_cfg
    53→

    55→# Default model lists per provider — used as fallback when the live
    56→# /models endpoint can't be reached.
    57→_DEFAULT_PROVIDER_MODELS = {
    58→    "zai": ["glm-5", "glm-4.7", "glm-4.5", "glm-4.5-flash"],
    59→    "kimi-coding": ["kimi-k2.5", "kimi-k2-thinking", "kimi-k2-turbo-preview"],
    60→    "minimax": ["MiniMax-M2.5", "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed", "MiniMax-M2.1"],

    65→def _setup_provider_model_selection(config, provider_id, current_model, prompt_choice, prompt_fn):
    66→    """Model selection for API-key providers with live /models detection.
    67→
    68→    Tries the provider's /models endpoint first.  Falls back to a
    69→    hardcoded default list with a warning if the endpoint is unreachable.
    70→    Always offers a 'Custom model' escape hatch.
    71→    """
    72→    from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY
    73→    from hermes_cli.config import get_env_value
    74→    from hermes_cli.models import fetch_api_models
    75→
    76→    pconfig = PROVIDER_REGISTRY[provider_id]
    77→
    78→    # Resolve API key and base URL for the probe
    79→    api_key = ""
    80→    for ev in pconfig.api_key_env_vars:
    81→        api_key = get_env_value(ev) or os.getenv(ev, "")
    82→        if api_key:
    83→            break
    84→    base_url_env = pconfig.base_url_env_var or ""
    85→    base_url = (get_env_value(base_url_env) if base_url_env else "") or pconfig.inference_base_url
    86→
    87→    # Try live /models endpoint
    88→    live_models = fetch_api_models(api_key, base_url)
    89→
    90→    if live_models:
    91→        provider_models = live_models
    92→        print_info(f"Found {len(live_models)} model(s) from {pconfig.name} API")
    93→    else:
    94→        provider_models = _DEFAULT_PROVIDER_MODELS.get(provider_id, [])
    95→        if provider_models:
    96→            print_warning(
    97→                f"Could not auto-detect models from {pconfig.name} API — showing defaults.\n"
    98→                f"    Use \"Custom model\" if the model you expect isn't listed."
    99→            )
   100→
```

```
    86→def _load_prefill_messages(file_path: str) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
    87→    """Load ephemeral prefill messages from a JSON file.
    88→    
    89→    The file should contain a JSON array of {role, content} dicts, e.g.:
    90→        [{"role": "user", "content": "Hi"}, {"role": "assistant", "content": "Hello!"}]
    91→    
    92→    Relative paths are resolved from ~/.hermes/.
    93→    Returns an empty list if the path is empty or the file doesn't exist.
    94→    """
    95→    if not file_path:
    96→        return []
    97→    path = Path(file_path).expanduser()
    98→    if not path.is_absolute():
    99→        path = Path.home() / ".hermes" / path
   100→    if not path.exists():
   101→        logger.warning("Prefill messages file not found: %s", path)
   102→        return []
   103→    try:
   104→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   105→            data = json.load(f)
   106→        if not isinstance(data, list):
   107→            logger.warning("Prefill messages file must contain a JSON array: %s", path)
   108→            return []
   109→        return data
   110→    except Exception as e:
   111→        logger.warning("Failed to load prefill messages from %s: %s", path, e)
   112→        return []
   113→
   114→
   115→def _parse_reasoning_config(effort: str) -> dict | None:
   116→    """Parse a reasoning effort level into an OpenRouter reasoning config dict.
   117→    
   118→    Valid levels: "xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal", "none".
   119→    Returns None to use the default (medium), or a config dict to override.
   120→    """
   121→    if not effort or not effort.strip():
   122→        return None
   123→    effort = effort.strip().lower()
   124→    if effort == "none":
   125→        return {"enabled": False}
   126→    valid = ("xhigh", "high", "medium", "low", "minimal")
   127→    if effort in valid:
   128→        return {"enabled": True, "effort": effort}
   129→    logger.warning("Unknown reasoning_effort '%s', using default (medium)", effort)
   130→    return None
   131→

   291→    if terminal_config.get("cwd") in (".", "auto", "cwd"):

   321→        # Sudo support (works with all backends)

   324→    
   325→    # Apply config values to env vars so terminal_tool picks them up.
   326→    # If the config file explicitly has a [terminal] section, those values are
   327→    # authoritative and override any .env settings.  When using defaults only
   328→    # (no config file or no terminal section), don't overwrite env vars that
   329→    # were already set by .env -- the user's .env is the fallback source.
   330→    for config_key, env_var in env_mappings.items():
   331→        if config_key in terminal_config:
   332→            if _file_has_terminal_config or env_var not in os.environ:
   333→                val = terminal_config[config_key]
   334→                if isinstance(val, list):
   335→                    import json
   336→                    os.environ[env_var] = json.dumps(val)
   337→                else:
   338→                    os.environ[env_var] = str(val)
   339→    
   340→    # Apply browser config to environment variables
   341→    browser_config = defaults.get("browser", {})
   342→    browser_env_mappings = {
   343→        "inactivity_timeout": "BROWSER_INACTIVITY_TIMEOUT",
   344→    }
   345→    
   346→    for config_key, env_var in browser_env_mappings.items():
   347→        if config_key in browser_config:
   348→            os.environ[env_var] = str(browser_config[config_key])
   349→    
   350→    # Apply compression config to environment variables
   351→    compression_config = defaults.get("compression", {})
   352→    compression_env_mappings = {
   353→        "enabled": "CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_ENABLED",
   354→        "threshold": "CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_THRESHOLD",
   355→        "summary_model": "CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_MODEL",
   356→        "summary_provider": "CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_PROVIDER",
   357→    }
   358→    
   359→    for config_key, env_var in compression_env_mappings.items():
   360→        if config_key in compression_config:
   361→            os.environ[env_var] = str(compression_config[config_key])
   362→    
   363→    # Apply auxiliary model overrides to environment variables.
   364→    # Vision and web_extract each have their own provider + model pair.
   365→    # (Compression is handled in the compression section above.)
   366→    # Only set env vars for non-empty / non-default values so auto-detection
   367→    # still works.
   368→    auxiliary_config = defaults.get("auxiliary", {})
   369→    auxiliary_task_env = {
   370→        # config key → (provider env var, model env var)
   371→        "vision":      ("AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER",      "AUXILIARY_VISION_MODEL"),
   372→        "web_extract": ("AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_PROVIDER",  "AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_MODEL"),
   373→    }
   374→    
   375→    for task_key, (prov_env, model_env) in auxiliary_task_env.items():
   376→        task_cfg = auxiliary_config.get(task_key, {})
   377→        if not isinstance(task_cfg, dict):
   378→            continue
   379→        prov = str(task_cfg.get("provider", "")).strip()
   380→        model = str(task_cfg.get("model", "")).strip()
   381→        if prov and prov != "auto":
   382→            os.environ[prov_env] = prov
   383→        if model:
   384→            os.environ[model_env] = model
   385→    
   386→    # Security settings
   387→    security_config = defaults.get("security", {})
   388→    if isinstance(security_config, dict):
   389→        redact = security_config.get("redact_secrets")
   390→        if redact is not None:
   391→            os.environ["HERMES_REDACT_SECRETS"] = str(redact).lower()
   392→
   393→    return defaults

   468→
   469→def _git_repo_root() -> Optional[str]:
   470→    """Return the git repo root for CWD, or None if not in a repo."""
   471→    import subprocess
   472→    try:
   473→        result = subprocess.run(
   474→            ["git", "rev-parse", "--show-toplevel"],
   475→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5,
   476→        )
   477→        if result.returncode == 0:
   478→            return result.stdout.strip()
   479→    except Exception:
   480→        pass
   481→    return None
   482→
   483→
   484→def _setup_worktree(repo_root: str = None) -> Optional[Dict[str, str]]:
   485→    """Create an isolated git worktree for this CLI session.
   486→
   487→    Returns a dict with worktree metadata on success, None on failure.
   488→    The dict contains: path, branch, repo_root.
   489→    """
   490→    import subprocess
   491→
   492→    repo_root = repo_root or _git_repo_root()
   493→    if not repo_root:
   494→        print("\033[31m✗ --worktree requires being inside a git repository.\033[0m")
   495→        print("  cd into your project repo first, then run hermes -w")
   496→        return None
```

```
1→"""Slash command definitions and autocomplete for the Hermes CLI.
     2→
     3→Contains the shared built-in ``COMMANDS`` dict and ``SlashCommandCompleter``.
     4→The completer can optionally include dynamic skill slash commands supplied by the
     5→interactive CLI.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→from collections.abc import Callable, Mapping
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→from prompt_toolkit.completion import Completer, Completion
    14→
    15→
    16→# Commands organized by category for better help display
    17→COMMANDS_BY_CATEGORY = {
    18→    "Session": {
    19→        "/new": "Start a new conversation (reset history)",
    20→        "/reset": "Reset conversation only (keep screen)",
    21→        "/clear": "Clear screen and reset conversation (fresh start)",
    22→        "/history": "Show conversation history",
    23→        "/save": "Save the current conversation",
    24→        "/retry": "Retry the last message (resend to agent)",
    25→        "/undo": "Remove the last user/assistant exchange",
    26→        "/title": "Set a title for the current session (usage: /title My Session Name)",
    27→        "/compress": "Manually compress conversation context (flush memories + summarize)",
    28→        "/rollback": "List or restore filesystem checkpoints (usage: /rollback [number])",
    29→        "/background": "Run a prompt in the background (usage: /background <prompt>)",
    30→    },
    31→    "Configuration": {
    32→        "/config": "Show current configuration",
    33→        "/model": "Show or change the current model",
    34→        "/provider": "Show available providers and current provider",
    35→        "/prompt": "View/set custom system prompt",
    36→        "/personality": "Set a predefined personality",
    37→        "/verbose": "Cycle tool progress display: off → new → all → verbose",
    38→        "/reasoning": "Manage reasoning effort and display (usage: /reasoning [level|show|hide])",
    39→        "/skin": "Show or change the display skin/theme",
    40→    },
    41→    "Tools & Skills": {
    42→        "/tools": "List available tools",
    43→        "/toolsets": "List available toolsets",
    44→        "/skills": "Search, install, inspect, or manage skills from online registries",
    45→        "/cron": "Manage scheduled tasks (list, add, remove)",
    46→        "/reload-mcp": "Reload MCP servers from config.yaml",
    47→    },
    48→    "Info": {
    49→        "/help": "Show this help message",
    50→        "/usage": "Show token usage for the current session",
    51→        "/insights": "Show usage insights and analytics (last 30 days)",
    52→        "/platforms": "Show gateway/messaging platform status",
    53→        "/paste": "Check clipboard for an image and attach it",
    54→    },
    55→    "Exit": {
    56→        "/quit": "Exit the CLI (also: /exit, /q)",
    57→    },
    58→}
    59→
    60→# Flat dict for backwards compatibility and autocomplete
    61→COMMANDS = {}
    62→for category_commands in COMMANDS_BY_CATEGORY.values():
    63→    COMMANDS.update(category_commands)
    64→
    65→
    66→class SlashCommandCompleter(Completer):
    67→    """Autocomplete for built-in slash commands and optional skill commands."""
    68→
    69→    def __init__(
    70→        self,
    71→        skill_commands_provider: Callable[[], Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]] | None = None,
    72→    ) -> None:
    73→        self._skill_commands_provider = skill_commands_provider
    74→
    75→    def _iter_skill_commands(self) -> Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    76→        if self._skill_commands_provider is None:
    77→            return {}
    78→        try:
    79→            return self._skill_commands_provider() or {}
    80→        except Exception:
    81→            return {}
    82→
    83→    @staticmethod
    84→    def _completion_text(cmd_name: str, word: str) -> str:
    85→        """Return replacement text for a completion.
    86→
    87→        When the user has already typed the full command exactly (``/help``),
    88→        returning ``help`` would be a no-op and prompt_toolkit suppresses the
    89→        menu. Appending a trailing space keeps the dropdown visible and makes
    90→        backspacing retrigger it naturally.
    91→        """
    92→        return f"{cmd_name} " if cmd_name == word else cmd_name
    93→
    94→    def get_completions(self, document, complete_event):
    95→        text = document.text_before_cursor
    96→        if not text.startswith("/"):
    97→            return
    98→
    99→        word = text[1:]
   100→
   101→        for cmd, desc in COMMANDS.items():
   102→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   103→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   104→                yield Completion(
   105→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   106→                    start_position=-len(word),
   107→                    display=cmd,
   108→                    display_meta=desc,
   109→                )
   110→
   111→        for cmd, info in self._iter_skill_commands().items():
   112→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   113→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   114→                description = str(info.get("description", "Skill command"))
   115→                short_desc = description[:50] + ("..." if len(description) > 50 else "")
   116→                yield Completion(
   117→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   118→                    start_position=-len(word),
   119→                    display=cmd,
   120→                    display_meta=f"⚡ {short_desc}",
   121→                )
   122→
```

```
   969→def load_env() -> Dict[str, str]:
   970→    """Load environment variables from ~/.hermes/.env."""
   971→    env_path = get_env_path()
   972→    env_vars = {}
   973→    
   974→    if env_path.exists():
   975→        # On Windows, open() defaults to the system locale (cp1252) which can
   976→        # fail on UTF-8 .env files. Use explicit UTF-8 only on Windows.
   977→        open_kw = {"encoding": "utf-8", "errors": "replace"} if _IS_WINDOWS else {}
   978→        with open(env_path, **open_kw) as f:
   979→            for line in f:
   980→                line = line.strip()
   981→                if line and not line.startswith('#') and '=' in line:
   982→                    key, _, value = line.partition('=')
   983→                    env_vars[key.strip()] = value.strip().strip('"\'')
   984→    
   985→    return env_vars
```

```
2150→def setup_tools(config: dict, first_install: bool = False):
  2151→    """Configure tools — delegates to the unified tools_command() in tools_config.py.
  2152→
  2153→    Both `hermes setup tools` and `hermes tools` use the same flow:
  2154→    platform selection → toolset toggles → provider/API key configuration.
  2155→
  2156→    Args:
  2157→        first_install: When True, uses the simplified first-install flow
  2158→            (no platform menu, prompts for all unconfigured API keys).
  2159→    """
  2160→    from hermes_cli.tools_config import tools_command
  2161→
  2162→    tools_command(first_install=first_install, config=config)
  2163→
  2164→
  2165→# =============================================================================
  2166→# OpenClaw Migration
  2167→# =============================================================================
  2168→
  2169→
  2170→_OPENCLAW_SCRIPT = (
  2171→    PROJECT_ROOT
  2172→    / "optional-skills"
  2173→    / "migration"
  2174→    / "openclaw-migration"
  2175→    / "scripts"
  2176→    / "openclaw_to_hermes.py"
  2177→)
  2178→
  2179→
  2180→def _offer_openclaw_migration(hermes_home: Path) -> bool:
  2181→    """Detect ~/.openclaw and offer to migrate during first-time setup.
  2182→
  2183→    Returns True if migration ran successfully, False otherwise.
  2184→    """
  2185→    openclaw_dir = Path.home() / ".openclaw"
  2186→    if not openclaw_dir.is_dir():
  2187→        return False
  2188→
  2189→    if not _OPENCLAW_SCRIPT.exists():
  2190→        return False
  2191→
  2192→    print()
  2193→    print_header("OpenClaw Installation Detected")
  2194→    print_info(f"Found OpenClaw data at {openclaw_dir}")
  2195→    print_info("Hermes can import your settings, memories, skills, and API keys.")
  2196→    print()
  2197→
  2198→    if not prompt_yes_no("Would you like to import from OpenClaw?", default=True):
  2199→        print_info(
  2200→            "Skipping migration. You can run it later via the openclaw-migration skill."
  2201→        )
  2202→        return False
  2203→
  2204→    # Ensure config.yaml exists before migration tries to read it
  2205→    config_path = get_config_path()
  2206→    if not config_path.exists():
  2207→        save_config(load_config())
  2208→
  2209→    # Dynamically load the migration script
  2210→    try:
  2211→        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(
  2212→            "openclaw_to_hermes", _OPENCLAW_SCRIPT
  2213→        )
  2214→        if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
  2215→            print_warning("Could not load migration script.")
  2216→            return False
  2217→
  2218→        mod = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
  2219→        # Register in sys.modules so @dataclass can resolve the module
  2220→        # (Python 3.11+ requires this for dynamically loaded modules)
  2221→        import sys as _sys
  2222→        _sys.modules[spec.name] = mod
  2223→        try:
  2224→            spec.loader.exec_module(mod)
  2225→        except Exception:
  2226→            _sys.modules.pop(spec.name, None)
  2227→            raise
  2228→
  2229→        # Run migration with the "full" preset, execute mode, no overwrite
  2230→        selected = mod.resolve_selected_options(None, None, preset="full")
  2231→        migrator = mod.Migrator(
  2232→            source_root=openclaw_dir.resolve(),
  2233→            target_root=hermes_home.resolve(),
  2234→            execute=True,
  2235→            workspace_target=None,
  2236→            overwrite=False,
  2237→            migrate_secrets=True,
  2238→            output_dir=None,
  2239→            selected_options=selected,
  2240→            preset_name="full",
  2241→        )
  2242→        report = migrator.migrate()
  2243→    except Exception as e:
  2244→        print_warning(f"Migration failed: {e}")
  2245→        logger.debug("OpenClaw migration error", exc_info=True)
  2246→        return False
  2247→
  2248→    # Print summary
  2249→    summary = report.get("summary", {})
```

```
1→"""Hermes CLI skin/theme engine.
     2→
     3→A data-driven skin system that lets users customize the CLI's visual appearance.
     4→Skins are defined as YAML files in ~/.hermes/skins/ or as built-in presets.
     5→No code changes are needed to add a new skin.
     6→
     7→SKIN YAML SCHEMA
     8→================
     9→
    10→All fields are optional. Missing values inherit from the ``default`` skin.
    11→
    12→.. code-block:: yaml
    13→
    14→    # Required: skin identity
    15→    name: mytheme                         # Unique skin name (lowercase, hyphens ok)
    16→    description: Short description        # Shown in /skin listing
    17→
    18→    # Colors: hex values for Rich markup (banner, UI, response box)
    19→    colors:
    20→      banner_border: "#CD7F32"            # Panel border color
    21→      banner_title: "#FFD700"             # Panel title text color
    22→      banner_accent: "#FFBF00"            # Section headers (Available Tools, etc.)
    23→      banner_dim: "#B8860B"               # Dim/muted text (separators, labels)
    24→      banner_text: "#FFF8DC"              # Body text (tool names, skill names)
    25→      ui_accent: "#FFBF00"               # General UI accent
    26→      ui_label: "#4dd0e1"                # UI labels
    27→      ui_ok: "#4caf50"                   # Success indicators
    28→      ui_error: "#ef5350"                # Error indicators
    29→      ui_warn: "#ffa726"                 # Warning indicators
    30→      prompt: "#FFF8DC"                  # Prompt text color
    31→      input_rule: "#CD7F32"              # Input area horizontal rule
    32→      response_border: "#FFD700"         # Response box border (ANSI)
    33→      session_label: "#DAA520"           # Session label color
    34→      session_border: "#8B8682"          # Session ID dim color
    35→
    36→    # Spinner: customize the animated spinner during API calls
    37→    spinner:
    38→      waiting_faces:                      # Faces shown while waiting for API
    39→        - "(⚔)"
    40→        - "(⛨)"
    41→      thinking_faces:                     # Faces shown during reasoning
    42→        - "(⌁)"
    43→        - "(<>)"
    44→      thinking_verbs:                     # Verbs for spinner messages
    45→        - "forging"
    46→        - "plotting"
    47→      wings:                              # Optional left/right spinner decorations
    48→        - ["⟪⚔", "⚔⟫"]                  # Each entry is [left, right] pair
    49→        - ["⟪▲", "▲⟫"]
    50→
    51→    # Branding: text strings used throughout the CLI
    52→    branding:
    53→      agent_name: "Hermes Agent"          # Banner title, status display
    54→      welcome: "Welcome message"          # Shown at CLI startup
    55→      goodbye: "Goodbye! ⚕"              # Shown on exit
    56→      response_label: " ⚕ Hermes "       # Response box header label
    57→      prompt_symbol: "❯ "                # Input prompt symbol
    58→      help_header: "(^_^)? Commands"      # /help header text
    59→
    60→    # Tool prefix: character for tool output lines (default: ┊)
    61→    tool_prefix: "┊"
    62→
    63→USAGE
    64→=====
    65→
    66→.. code-block:: python
    67→
    68→    from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin, list_skins, set_active_skin
    69→
    70→    skin = get_active_skin()
    71→    print(skin.colors["banner_title"])    # "#FFD700"
    72→    print(skin.get_branding("agent_name"))  # "Hermes Agent"
    73→
    74→    set_active_skin("ares")               # Switch to built-in ares skin
    75→    set_active_skin("mytheme")            # Switch to user skin from ~/.hermes/skins/
    76→
    77→BUILT-IN SKINS
    78→==============
    79→
    80→- ``default`` — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii (the current look)
    81→- ``ares``    — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with custom spinner wings
    82→- ``mono``    — Clean grayscale monochrome
    83→- ``slate``   — Cool blue developer-focused theme
    84→
    85→USER SKINS
    86→==========
    87→
    88→Drop a YAML file in ``~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`` following the schema above.
    89→Activate with ``/skin <name>`` in the CLI or ``display.skin: <name>`` in config.yaml.
    90→"""
    91→
    92→import logging
    93→import os
    94→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    95→from pathlib import Path
    96→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
    97→
    98→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    99→
   100→
   101→# =============================================================================
   102→# Skin data structure
   103→# =============================================================================
   104→
   105→@dataclass
   106→class SkinConfig:
   107→    """Complete skin configuration."""
   108→    name: str
   109→    description: str = ""
   110→    colors: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   111→    spinner: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)

   115→    banner_hero: str = ""    # Rich-markup hero art (replaces HERMES_CADUCEUS)
   116→
   117→    def get_color(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   118→        """Get a color value with fallback."""
   119→        return self.colors.get(key, fallback)
   120→
   121→    def get_spinner_list(self, key: str) -> List[str]:
   122→        """Get a spinner list (faces, verbs, etc.)."""
   123→        return self.spinner.get(key, [])
   124→
   125→    def get_spinner_wings(self) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
   126→        """Get spinner wing pairs, or empty list if none."""
   127→        raw = self.spinner.get("wings", [])
   128→        result = []
   129→        for pair in raw:

   131→                result.append((str(pair[0]), str(pair[1])))
   132→        return result

   134→    def get_branding(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   135→        """Get a branding value with fallback."""
   136→        return self.branding.get(key, fallback)

   499→# =============================================================================
   500→# Skin loading and management
   501→# =============================================================================
   502→
   503→_active_skin: Optional[SkinConfig] = None
   504→_active_skin_name: str = "default"
   505→
   506→
   507→def _skins_dir() -> Path:
   508→    """User skins directory."""
   509→    home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   510→    return home / "skins"
   511→
   512→
   513→def _load_skin_from_yaml(path: Path) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   514→    """Load a skin definition from a YAML file."""
   515→    try:
   516→        import yaml
   517→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   518→            data = yaml.safe_load(f)
   519→        if isinstance(data, dict) and "name" in data:
   520→            return data
   521→    except Exception as e:
   522→        logger.debug("Failed to load skin from %s: %s", path, e)
   523→    return None
   524→
   525→
   526→def _build_skin_config(data: Dict[str, Any]) -> SkinConfig:
   527→    """Build a SkinConfig from a raw dict (built-in or loaded from YAML)."""
   528→    # Start with default values as base for missing keys
   529→    default = _BUILTIN_SKINS["default"]
   530→    colors = dict(default.get("colors", {}))
   531→    colors.update(data.get("colors", {}))
   532→    spinner = dict(default.get("spinner", {}))
   533→    spinner.update(data.get("spinner", {}))
   534→    branding = dict(default.get("branding", {}))
   535→    branding.update(data.get("branding", {}))
   536→
   537→    return SkinConfig(
   538→        name=data.get("name", "unknown"),
   539→        description=data.get("description", ""),
   540→        colors=colors,
   541→        spinner=spinner,
   542→        branding=branding,
   543→        tool_prefix=data.get("tool_prefix", default.get("tool_prefix", "┊")),
   544→        banner_logo=data.get("banner_logo", ""),
   545→        banner_hero=data.get("banner_hero", ""),
   546→    )
   547→
   548→
   549→def list_skins() -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
   550→    """List all available skins (built-in + user-installed).
   551→
   552→    Returns list of {"name": ..., "description": ..., "source": "builtin"|"user"}.
   553→    """
   554→    result = []
   555→    for name, data in _BUILTIN_SKINS.items():
   556→        result.append({
   557→            "name": name,
   558→            "description": data.get("description", ""),
   559→            "source": "builtin",
   560→        })
   561→
   562→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   563→    if skins_path.is_dir():
   564→        for f in sorted(skins_path.glob("*.yaml")):
   565→            data = _load_skin_from_yaml(f)
   566→            if data:
   567→                skin_name = data.get("name", f.stem)
   568→                # Skip if it shadows a built-in
   569→                if any(s["name"] == skin_name for s in result):
   570→                    continue
   571→                result.append({
   572→                    "name": skin_name,
   573→                    "description": data.get("description", ""),
   574→                    "source": "user",
   575→                })
   576→
   577→    return result
   578→
   579→
   580→def load_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   581→    """Load a skin by name. Checks user skins first, then built-in."""
   582→    # Check user skins directory
   583→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   584→    user_file = skins_path / f"{name}.yaml"
   585→    if user_file.is_file():
   586→        data = _load_skin_from_yaml(user_file)
   587→        if data:
   588→            return _build_skin_config(data)
   589→
   590→    # Check built-in skins
   591→    if name in _BUILTIN_SKINS:
   592→        return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS[name])
   593→
   594→    # Fallback to default
   595→    logger.warning("Skin '%s' not found, using default", name)
   596→    return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS["default"])
   597→
   598→
   599→def get_active_skin() -> SkinConfig:
   600→    """Get the currently active skin config (cached)."""
   601→    global _active_skin
   602→    if _active_skin is None:
   603→        _active_skin = load_skin(_active_skin_name)
   604→    return _active_skin
   605→
   606→
   607→def set_active_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   608→    """Switch the active skin. Returns the new SkinConfig."""
   609→    global _active_skin, _active_skin_name
   610→    _active_skin_name = name
   611→    _active_skin = load_skin(name)
   612→    return _active_skin
   613→
   614→
   615→def get_active_skin_name() -> str:
   616→    """Get the name of the currently active skin."""
   617→    return _active_skin_name
   618→
   619→
   620→def init_skin_from_config(config: dict) -> None:
   621→    """Initialize the active skin from CLI config at startup.
   622→
   623→    Call this once during CLI init with the loaded config dict.
   624→    """
   625→    display = config.get("display", {})
   626→    skin_name = display.get("skin", "default")
   627→    if isinstance(skin_name, str) and skin_name.strip():
   628→        set_active_skin(skin_name.strip())
   629→    else:
   630→        set_active_skin("default")
   631→
```

```
   566→def _cleanup_worktree(info: Dict[str, str] = None) -> None:
   567→    """Remove a worktree and its branch on exit.
   568→
   569→    If the worktree has uncommitted changes, warn and keep it.
   570→    """
   571→    global _active_worktree
   572→    info = info or _active_worktree
   573→    if not info:
   574→        return
   575→
   576→    import subprocess
   577→
   578→    wt_path = info["path"]
   579→    branch = info["branch"]
   580→    repo_root = info["repo_root"]
   581→
   582→    if not Path(wt_path).exists():
   583→        return
   584→
   585→    # Check for uncommitted changes
   586→    try:
   587→        status = subprocess.run(
   588→            ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
   589→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10, cwd=wt_path,
   590→        )
   591→        has_changes = bool(status.stdout.strip())
   592→    except Exception:
   593→        has_changes = True  # Assume dirty on error — don't delete
   594→
   595→    if has_changes:
   596→        print(f"\n\033[33m⚠ Worktree has uncommitted changes, keeping: {wt_path}\033[0m")
   597→        print(f"  To clean up manually: git worktree remove {wt_path}")
   598→        _active_worktree = None
   599→        return
   600→
   601→    # Remove worktree
   602→    try:
   603→        subprocess.run(
   604→            ["git", "worktree", "remove", wt_path, "--force"],
   605→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=15, cwd=repo_root,
   606→        )
   607→    except Exception as e:
   608→        logger.debug("Failed to remove worktree: %s", e)
   609→
   610→    # Delete the branch (only if it was never pushed / has no upstream)
   611→    try:
   612→        subprocess.run(
   613→            ["git", "branch", "-D", branch],
   614→            capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10, cwd=repo_root,
   615→        )
   616→    except Exception as e:
   617→        logger.debug("Failed to delete branch %s: %s", branch, e)
   618→
   619→    _active_worktree = None
   620→    print(f"\033[32m✓ Worktree cleaned up: {wt_path}\033[0m")

   622→
   623→def _prune_stale_worktrees(repo_root: str, max_age_hours: int = 24) -> None:
   624→    """Remove worktrees older than max_age_hours that have no uncommitted changes.
   625→
   626→    Runs silently on startup to clean up after crashed/killed sessions.
   627→    """
   628→    import subprocess
   629→    import time
   630→
   631→    worktrees_dir = Path(repo_root) / ".worktrees"
   632→    if not worktrees_dir.exists():
   633→        return
   634→
   635→    now = time.time()
   636→    cutoff = now - (max_age_hours * 3600)
   637→
   638→    for entry in worktrees_dir.iterdir():
   639→        if not entry.is_dir() or not entry.name.startswith("hermes-"):
   640→            continue
   641→
   642→        # Check age
   643→        try:
   644→            mtime = entry.stat().st_mtime
   645→            if mtime > cutoff:
   646→                continue  # Too recent — skip
   647→        except Exception:
   648→            continue
   649→
   650→        # Check for uncommitted changes
   651→        try:
   652→            status = subprocess.run(
   653→                ["git", "status", "--porcelain"],
   654→                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5, cwd=str(entry),
   655→            )
   656→            if status.stdout.strip():
   657→                continue  # Has changes — skip
   658→        except Exception:
   659→            continue  # Can't check — skip
   660→
   661→        # Safe to remove
   662→        try:
   663→            branch_result = subprocess.run(
   664→                ["git", "branch", "--show-current"],
   665→                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5, cwd=str(entry),
   666→            )
   667→            branch = branch_result.stdout.strip()
   668→
   669→            subprocess.run(
   670→                ["git", "worktree", "remove", str(entry), "--force"],
   671→                capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=15, cwd=repo_root,
   672→            )
   673→            if branch:
   674→                subprocess.run(
   675→                    ["git", "branch", "-D", branch],
   676→                    capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=10, cwd=repo_root,
   677→                )
   678→            logger.debug("Pruned stale worktree: %s", entry.name)
   679→        except Exception as e:
   680→            logger.debug("Failed to prune worktree %s: %s", entry.name, e)
   681→

   699→def _cprint(text: str):
   700→    """Print ANSI-colored text through prompt_toolkit's native renderer.
   701→
   702→    Raw ANSI escapes written via print() are swallowed by patch_stdout's
   703→    StdoutProxy.  Routing through print_formatted_text(ANSI(...)) lets
   704→    prompt_toolkit parse the escapes and render real colors.
   705→    """
   706→    _pt_print(_PT_ANSI(text))
   707→
   708→
   709→class ChatConsole:
   710→    """Rich Console adapter for prompt_toolkit's patch_stdout context.
   711→
   712→    Captures Rich's rendered ANSI output and routes it through _cprint
   713→    so colors and markup render correctly inside the interactive chat loop.
   714→    Drop-in replacement for Rich Console — just pass this to any function
   715→    that expects a console.print() interface.
   716→    """
   717→
   718→    def __init__(self):
   719→        from io import StringIO
   720→        self._buffer = StringIO()
   721→        self._inner = Console(file=self._buffer, force_terminal=True, highlight=False)
   722→
   723→    def print(self, *args, **kwargs):
   724→        self._buffer.seek(0)
   725→        self._buffer.truncate()
   726→        # Read terminal width at render time so panels adapt to current size
   727→        self._inner.width = shutil.get_terminal_size((80, 24)).columns
   728→        self._inner.print(*args, **kwargs)
   729→        output = self._buffer.getvalue()
   730→        for line in output.rstrip("\n").split("\n"):
   731→            _cprint(line)
```
