   742→HERMES_CADUCEUS = """[#CD7F32]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⢀⣀⡀⠀⣀⣀⠀⢀⣀⡀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   743→[#CD7F32]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⢀⣠⣴⣾⣿⣿⣇⠸⣿⣿⠇⣸⣿⣿⣷⣦⣄⡀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   744→[#FFBF00]⠀⢀⣠⣴⣶⠿⠋⣩⡿⣿⡿⠻⣿⡇⢠⡄⢸⣿⠟⢿⣿⢿⣍⠙⠿⣶⣦⣄⡀⠀[/]
   745→[#FFBF00]⠀⠀⠉⠉⠁⠶⠟⠋⠀⠉⠀⢀⣈⣁⡈⢁⣈⣁⡀⠀⠉⠀⠙⠻⠶⠈⠉⠉⠀⠀[/]
   746→[#FFD700]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⣴⣿⡿⠛⢁⡈⠛⢿⣿⣦⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   747→[#FFD700]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠿⣿⣦⣤⣈⠁⢠⣴⣿⠿⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   748→[#FFBF00]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠈⠉⠻⢿⣿⣦⡉⠁⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   749→[#FFBF00]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠘⢷⣦⣈⠛⠃⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   750→[#CD7F32]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⢠⣴⠦⠈⠙⠿⣦⡄⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   751→[#CD7F32]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠸⣿⣤⡈⠁⢤⣿⠇⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   752→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠉⠛⠷⠄⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   753→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⢀⣀⠑⢶⣄⡀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   754→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⣿⠁⢰⡆⠈⡿⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   755→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠈⠳⠈⣡⠞⠁⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]
   756→[#B8860B]⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠈⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀⠀[/]"""
   757→
   758→# Compact banner for smaller terminals (fallback)
   759→# Note: built dynamically by _build_compact_banner() to fit terminal width
   760→COMPACT_BANNER = """
   761→[bold #FFD700]╔══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╗[/]
   762→[bold #FFD700]║[/]  [#FFBF00]⚕ NOUS HERMES[/] [dim #B8860B]- AI Agent Framework[/]              [bold #FFD700]║[/]
   763→[bold #FFD700]║[/]  [#CD7F32]Messenger of the Digital Gods[/]    [dim #B8860B]Nous Research[/]   [bold #FFD700]║[/]
   764→[bold #FFD700]╚══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════╝[/]
   765→"""
   766→
   767→
   768→def _build_compact_banner() -> str:
   769→    """Build a compact banner that fits the current terminal width."""
   770→    w = min(shutil.get_terminal_size().columns - 2, 64)
   771→    if w < 30:
   772→        return "\n[#FFBF00]⚕ NOUS HERMES[/] [dim #B8860B]- Nous Research[/]\n"
   773→    inner = w - 2  # inside the box border
   774→    bar = "═" * w
   775→    line1 = "⚕ NOUS HERMES - AI Agent Framework"
   776→    line2 = "Messenger of the Digital Gods  ·  Nous Research"
   777→    # Truncate and pad to fit
   778→    line1 = line1[:inner - 2].ljust(inner - 2)
   779→    line2 = line2[:inner - 2].ljust(inner - 2)
   780→    return (
   781→        f"\n[bold #FFD700]╔{bar}╗[/]\n"
   782→        f"[bold #FFD700]║[/] [#FFBF00]{line1}[/] [bold #FFD700]║[/]\n"
   783→        f"[bold #FFD700]║[/] [dim #B8860B]{line2}[/] [bold #FFD700]║[/]\n"
   784→        f"[bold #FFD700]╚{bar}╝[/]\n"
   785→    )
   786→
   787→
   788→def _get_available_skills() -> Dict[str, List[str]]:
   789→    """
   790→    Scan ~/.hermes/skills/ and return skills grouped by category.
   791→    
   792→    Returns:
   793→        Dict mapping category name to list of skill names
   794→    """
   795→    import os
   796→    
   797→    hermes_home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   798→    skills_dir = hermes_home / "skills"
   799→    skills_by_category = {}
   800→    
   801→    if not skills_dir.exists():
   802→        return skills_by_category
   803→    
   804→    for skill_file in skills_dir.rglob("SKILL.md"):
   805→        rel_path = skill_file.relative_to(skills_dir)
   806→        parts = rel_path.parts
   807→        
   808→        if len(parts) >= 2:
   809→            category = parts[0]
   810→            skill_name = parts[-2]
   811→        else:
   812→            category = "general"
   813→            skill_name = skill_file.parent.name
   814→        
   815→        skills_by_category.setdefault(category, []).append(skill_name)
   816→    
   817→    return skills_by_category
   818→
   819→
   820→def _format_context_length(tokens: int) -> str:
   821→    """Format a token count for display (e.g. 128000 → '128K', 1048576 → '1M')."""
   822→    if tokens >= 1_000_000:
   823→        val = tokens / 1_000_000
   824→        return f"{val:g}M"
   825→    elif tokens >= 1_000:
   826→        val = tokens / 1_000
   827→        return f"{val:g}K"
   828→    return str(tokens)
   829→
   830→
   831→def build_welcome_banner(console: Console, model: str, cwd: str, tools: List[dict] = None, enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None, session_id: str = None, context_length: int = None):
   832→    """
   833→    Build and print a Claude Code-style welcome banner with caduceus on left and info on right.
   834→    
   835→    Args:
   836→        console: Rich Console instance for printing
   837→        model: The current model name (e.g., "anthropic/claude-opus-4")
   838→        cwd: Current working directory
   839→        tools: List of tool definitions
   840→        enabled_toolsets: List of enabled toolset names
   841→        session_id: Unique session identifier for logging
   842→        context_length: Model's context window size in tokens
   843→    """
   844→    from model_tools import check_tool_availability, TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS
   845→    
   846→    tools = tools or []
   847→    enabled_toolsets = enabled_toolsets or []
   848→    
   849→    # Get unavailable tools info for coloring
   850→    _, unavailable_toolsets = check_tool_availability(quiet=True)
   851→    disabled_tools = set()
   852→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   853→        disabled_tools.update(item.get("tools", []))
   854→    
   855→    # Build the side-by-side content using a table for precise control
   856→    layout_table = Table.grid(padding=(0, 2))
   857→    layout_table.add_column("left", justify="center")
   858→    layout_table.add_column("right", justify="left")
   859→    
   860→    # Build left content: caduceus + model info
   861→    # Resolve skin colors for the banner
   862→    try:
   863→        from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin
   864→        _bskin = get_active_skin()
   865→        _accent = _bskin.get_color("banner_accent", "#FFBF00")
   866→        _dim = _bskin.get_color("banner_dim", "#B8860B")
   867→        _text = _bskin.get_color("banner_text", "#FFF8DC")
   868→        _session_c = _bskin.get_color("session_border", "#8B8682")
   869→        _title_c = _bskin.get_color("banner_title", "#FFD700")
   870→        _border_c = _bskin.get_color("banner_border", "#CD7F32")
   871→        _agent_name = _bskin.get_branding("agent_name", "Hermes Agent")
   872→    except Exception:
   873→        _bskin = None
   874→        _accent, _dim, _text = "#FFBF00", "#B8860B", "#FFF8DC"
   875→        _session_c, _title_c, _border_c = "#8B8682", "#FFD700", "#CD7F32"
   876→        _agent_name = "Hermes Agent"
   877→
   878→    _hero = _bskin.banner_hero if hasattr(_bskin, 'banner_hero') and _bskin.banner_hero else HERMES_CADUCEUS
   879→    left_lines = ["", _hero, ""]
   880→    
   881→    # Shorten model name for display
   882→    model_short = model.split("/")[-1] if "/" in model else model
   883→    if len(model_short) > 28:
   884→        model_short = model_short[:25] + "..."
   885→    
   886→    ctx_str = f" [dim {_dim}]·[/] [dim {_dim}]{_format_context_length(context_length)} context[/]" if context_length else ""
   887→    left_lines.append(f"[{_accent}]{model_short}[/]{ctx_str} [dim {_dim}]·[/] [dim {_dim}]Nous Research[/]")
   888→    left_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}]{cwd}[/]")
   889→    
   890→    # Add session ID if provided
   891→    if session_id:
   892→        left_lines.append(f"[dim {_session_c}]Session: {session_id}[/]")
   893→    left_content = "\n".join(left_lines)
   894→    
   895→    # Build right content: tools list grouped by toolset
   896→    right_lines = []
   897→    right_lines.append(f"[bold {_accent}]Available Tools[/]")
   898→    
   899→    # Group tools by toolset (include all possible tools, both enabled and disabled)
   900→    toolsets_dict = {}
   901→    
   902→    # First, add all enabled tools
   903→    for tool in tools:
   904→        tool_name = tool["function"]["name"]
   905→        toolset = get_toolset_for_tool(tool_name) or "other"
   906→        if toolset not in toolsets_dict:
   907→            toolsets_dict[toolset] = []
   908→        toolsets_dict[toolset].append(tool_name)
   909→    
   910→    # Also add disabled toolsets so they show in the banner
   911→    for item in unavailable_toolsets:
   912→        # Map the internal toolset ID to display name
   913→        toolset_id = item.get("id", item.get("name", "unknown"))
   914→        display_name = f"{toolset_id}_tools" if not toolset_id.endswith("_tools") else toolset_id
   915→        if display_name not in toolsets_dict:
   916→            toolsets_dict[display_name] = []
   917→        for tool_name in item.get("tools", []):
   918→            if tool_name not in toolsets_dict[display_name]:
   919→                toolsets_dict[display_name].append(tool_name)
   920→    
   921→    # Display tools grouped by toolset (compact format, max 8 groups)
   922→    sorted_toolsets = sorted(toolsets_dict.keys())
   923→    display_toolsets = sorted_toolsets[:8]
   924→    remaining_toolsets = len(sorted_toolsets) - 8
   925→    
   926→    for toolset in display_toolsets:
   927→        tool_names = toolsets_dict[toolset]
   928→        # Color each tool name - red if disabled, normal if enabled
   929→        colored_names = []
   930→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
   931→            if name in disabled_tools:
   932→                colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   933→            else:
   934→                colored_names.append(f"[{_text}]{name}[/]")
   935→        
   936→        tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   937→        # Truncate if too long (accounting for markup)
   938→        if len(", ".join(sorted(tool_names))) > 45:
   939→            # Rebuild with truncation
   940→            short_names = []
   941→            length = 0
   942→            for name in sorted(tool_names):
   943→                if length + len(name) + 2 > 42:
   944→                    short_names.append("...")
   945→                    break
   946→                short_names.append(name)
   947→                length += len(name) + 2
   948→            # Re-color the truncated list
   949→            colored_names = []
   950→            for name in short_names:
   951→                if name == "...":
   952→                    colored_names.append("[dim]...[/]")
   953→                elif name in disabled_tools:
   954→                    colored_names.append(f"[red]{name}[/]")
   955→                else:
   956→                    colored_names.append(f"[{_text}]{name}[/]")
   957→            tools_str = ", ".join(colored_names)
   958→        
   959→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}]{toolset}:[/] {tools_str}")
   960→    
   961→    if remaining_toolsets > 0:
   962→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}](and {remaining_toolsets} more toolsets...)[/]")
   963→    
   964→    right_lines.append("")
   965→    
   966→    # Add skills section
   967→    right_lines.append(f"[bold {_accent}]Available Skills[/]")
   968→    skills_by_category = _get_available_skills()
   969→    total_skills = sum(len(s) for s in skills_by_category.values())
   970→    
   971→    if skills_by_category:
   972→        for category in sorted(skills_by_category.keys()):
   973→            skill_names = sorted(skills_by_category[category])
   974→            # Show first 8 skills, then "..." if more
   975→            if len(skill_names) > 8:
   976→                display_names = skill_names[:8]
   977→                skills_str = ", ".join(display_names) + f" +{len(skill_names) - 8} more"
   978→            else:
   979→                skills_str = ", ".join(skill_names)
   980→            # Truncate if still too long
   981→            if len(skills_str) > 50:
   982→                skills_str = skills_str[:47] + "..."
   983→            right_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}]{category}:[/] [{_text}]{skills_str}[/]")
   984→    else:
   985→        right_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}]No skills installed[/]")
   986→    
   987→    right_lines.append("")
   988→    right_lines.append(f"[dim {_dim}]{len(tools)} tools · {total_skills} skills · /help for commands[/]")
   989→    
   990→    right_content = "\n".join(right_lines)
   991→    
   992→    # Add to table
   993→    layout_table.add_row(left_content, right_content)
   994→    
   995→    # Wrap in a panel with the title
   996→    outer_panel = Panel(
   997→        layout_table,
   998→        title=f"[bold {_title_c}]{_agent_name} v{VERSION} ({RELEASE_DATE})[/]",
   999→        border_style=_border_c,
  1000→        padding=(0, 2),
  1001→    )
  1002→    
  1003→    # Print the big logo — use skin's custom logo if available
  1004→    console.print()
  1005→    term_width = shutil.get_terminal_size().columns
  1006→    if term_width >= 95:
  1007→        _logo = _bskin.banner_logo if hasattr(_bskin, 'banner_logo') and _bskin.banner_logo else HERMES_AGENT_LOGO
  1008→        console.print(_logo)
  1009→        console.print()
  1010→    
  1011→    # Print the panel with caduceus and info
  1012→    console.print(outer_panel)
  1013→
  1014→

  1019→from agent.skill_commands import scan_skill_commands, get_skill_commands, build_skill_invocation_message
  1020→
  1021→_skill_commands = scan_skill_commands()

  1023→
  1024→def save_config_value(key_path: str, value: any) -> bool:
  1025→    """
  1026→    Save a value to the active config file at the specified key path.
  1027→    
  1028→    Respects the same lookup order as load_cli_config():
  1029→    1. ~/.hermes/config.yaml (user config - preferred, used if it exists)
  1030→    2. ./cli-config.yaml (project config - fallback)
  1031→    
  1032→    Args:
  1033→        key_path: Dot-separated path like "agent.system_prompt"
  1034→        value: Value to save
  1035→    
  1036→    Returns:
  1037→        True if successful, False otherwise
  1038→    """
  1039→    # Use the same precedence as load_cli_config: user config first, then project config
  1040→    user_config_path = Path.home() / '.hermes' / 'config.yaml'
  1041→    project_config_path = Path(__file__).parent / 'cli-config.yaml'
  1042→    config_path = user_config_path if user_config_path.exists() else project_config_path
  1043→    
  1044→    try:
  1045→        # Ensure parent directory exists (for ~/.hermes/config.yaml on first use)
  1046→        config_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  1047→        
  1048→        # Load existing config
  1049→        if config_path.exists():
  1050→            with open(config_path, 'r') as f:
  1051→                config = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1052→        else:
  1053→            config = {}
  1054→        
  1055→        # Navigate to the key and set value
  1056→        keys = key_path.split('.')
  1057→        current = config
  1058→        for key in keys[:-1]:
  1059→            if key not in current or not isinstance(current[key], dict):
  1060→                current[key] = {}
  1061→            current = current[key]
  1062→        current[keys[-1]] = value
  1063→        
  1064→        # Save back
  1065→        with open(config_path, 'w') as f:
  1066→            yaml.dump(config, f, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)
  1067→        
  1068→        # Enforce owner-only permissions on config files (contain API keys)
  1069→        try:
  1070→            os.chmod(config_path, 0o600)
  1071→        except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
  1072→            pass
  1073→        
  1074→        return True
  1075→    except Exception as e:
  1076→        logger.error("Failed to save config: %s", e)
  1077→        return False
  1078→
  1079→
  1080→# ============================================================================
  1081→# HermesCLI Class
  1082→# ============================================================================
  1083→
  1084→class HermesCLI:
  1085→    """
  1086→    Interactive CLI for the Hermes Agent.
  1087→    
  1088→    Provides a REPL interface with rich formatting, command history,
  1089→    and tool execution capabilities.
  1090→    """
  1091→    
  1092→    def __init__(
  1093→        self,
  1094→        model: str = None,
  1095→        toolsets: List[str] = None,
  1096→        provider: str = None,
  1097→        api_key: str = None,
  1098→        base_url: str = None,
  1099→        max_turns: int = None,
  1100→        verbose: bool = False,
  1101→        compact: bool = False,
  1102→        resume: str = None,
  1103→        checkpoints: bool = False,
  1104→        pass_session_id: bool = False,
  1105→    ):
  1106→        """
  1107→        Initialize the Hermes CLI.
  1108→
  1109→        Args:
  1110→            model: Model to use (default: from env or claude-sonnet)
  1111→            toolsets: List of toolsets to enable (default: all)
  1112→            provider: Inference provider ("auto", "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex", "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn")
  1113→            api_key: API key (default: from environment)
  1114→            base_url: API base URL (default: OpenRouter)
  1115→            max_turns: Maximum tool-calling iterations shared with subagents (default: 90)
  1116→            verbose: Enable verbose logging
  1117→            compact: Use compact display mode
  1118→            resume: Session ID to resume (restores conversation history from SQLite)
  1119→            pass_session_id: Include the session ID in the agent's system prompt
  1120→        """
  1121→        # Initialize Rich console
  1122→        self.console = Console()
  1123→        self.config = CLI_CONFIG
  1124→        self.compact = compact if compact is not None else CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("compact", False)
  1125→        # tool_progress: "off", "new", "all", "verbose" (from config.yaml display section)
  1126→        self.tool_progress_mode = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("tool_progress", "all")
  1127→        # resume_display: "full" (show history) | "minimal" (one-liner only)
  1128→        self.resume_display = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("resume_display", "full")
  1129→        # bell_on_complete: play terminal bell (\a) when agent finishes a response
  1130→        self.bell_on_complete = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("bell_on_complete", False)
  1131→        # show_reasoning: display model thinking/reasoning before the response
  1132→        self.show_reasoning = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("show_reasoning", False)
  1133→        self.verbose = verbose if verbose is not None else (self.tool_progress_mode == "verbose")
  1134→        
  1135→        # Configuration - priority: CLI args > env vars > config file
  1136→        # Model comes from: CLI arg or config.yaml (single source of truth).
  1137→        # LLM_MODEL/OPENAI_MODEL env vars are NOT checked — config.yaml is
  1138→        # authoritative.  This avoids conflicts in multi-agent setups where
  1139→        # env vars would stomp each other.
  1140→        _model_config = CLI_CONFIG.get("model", {})
  1141→        _config_model = _model_config.get("default", "") if isinstance(_model_config, dict) else (_model_config or "")
  1142→        self.model = model or _config_model or "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6"
  1143→        # Track whether model was explicitly chosen by the user or fell back
  1144→        # to the global default.  Provider-specific normalisation may override
  1145→        # the default silently but should warn when overriding an explicit choice.
  1146→        self._model_is_default = not model
  1147→
  1148→        self._explicit_api_key = api_key
  1149→        self._explicit_base_url = base_url
  1150→
  1151→        # Provider selection is resolved lazily at use-time via _ensure_runtime_credentials().
  1152→        self.requested_provider = (
  1153→            provider
  1154→            or os.getenv("HERMES_INFERENCE_PROVIDER")
  1155→            or CLI_CONFIG["model"].get("provider")
  1156→            or "auto"
  1157→        )
  1158→        self._provider_source: Optional[str] = None
  1159→        self.provider = self.requested_provider
  1160→        self.api_mode = "chat_completions"
  1161→        self.base_url = (
  1162→            base_url
  1163→            or os.getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL")
  1164→            or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_BASE_URL", CLI_CONFIG["model"]["base_url"])
  1165→        )
  1166→        # Match key to resolved base_url: OpenRouter URL → prefer OPENROUTER_API_KEY,
  1167→        # custom endpoint → prefer OPENAI_API_KEY (issue #560).
  1168→        # Note: _ensure_runtime_credentials() re-resolves this before first use.
  1169→        if "openrouter.ai" in self.base_url:
  1170→            self.api_key = api_key or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY") or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
  1171→        else:
  1172→            self.api_key = api_key or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY") or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
  1173→        self._nous_key_expires_at: Optional[str] = None
  1174→        self._nous_key_source: Optional[str] = None
  1175→        # Max turns priority: CLI arg > config file > env var > default
  1176→        if max_turns is not None:  # CLI arg was explicitly set
  1177→            self.max_turns = max_turns
  1178→        elif CLI_CONFIG["agent"].get("max_turns"):
  1179→            self.max_turns = CLI_CONFIG["agent"]["max_turns"]
  1180→        elif CLI_CONFIG.get("max_turns"):  # Backwards compat: root-level max_turns
  1181→            self.max_turns = CLI_CONFIG["max_turns"]
  1182→        elif os.getenv("HERMES_MAX_ITERATIONS"):
  1183→            self.max_turns = int(os.getenv("HERMES_MAX_ITERATIONS"))
  1184→        else:
  1185→            self.max_turns = 90
  1186→        
  1187→        # Parse and validate toolsets
  1188→        self.enabled_toolsets = toolsets
  1189→        if toolsets and "all" not in toolsets and "*" not in toolsets:
  1190→            # Validate each toolset
  1191→            invalid = [t for t in toolsets if not validate_toolset(t)]
  1192→            if invalid:
  1193→                self.console.print(f"[bold red]Warning: Unknown toolsets: {', '.join(invalid)}[/]")
  1194→        
  1195→        # Filesystem checkpoints: CLI flag > config
  1196→        cp_cfg = CLI_CONFIG.get("checkpoints", {})
  1197→        if isinstance(cp_cfg, bool):
  1198→            cp_cfg = {"enabled": cp_cfg}
  1199→        self.checkpoints_enabled = checkpoints or cp_cfg.get("enabled", False)
  1200→        self.checkpoint_max_snapshots = cp_cfg.get("max_snapshots", 50)
  1201→        self.pass_session_id = pass_session_id
  1202→        
  1203→        # Ephemeral system prompt: env var takes precedence, then config
  1204→        self.system_prompt = (
  1205→            os.getenv("HERMES_EPHEMERAL_SYSTEM_PROMPT", "")
  1206→            or CLI_CONFIG["agent"].get("system_prompt", "")
  1207→        )
  1208→        self.personalities = CLI_CONFIG["agent"].get("personalities", {})
  1209→        
  1210→        # Ephemeral prefill messages (few-shot priming, never persisted)
  1211→        self.prefill_messages = _load_prefill_messages(
  1212→            CLI_CONFIG["agent"].get("prefill_messages_file", "")
  1213→        )

  1216→        self.reasoning_config = _parse_reasoning_config(
  1217→            CLI_CONFIG["agent"].get("reasoning_effort", "")
  1218→        )
  1219→        
  1220→        # OpenRouter provider routing preferences
  1221→        pr = CLI_CONFIG.get("provider_routing", {}) or {}
  1222→        self._provider_sort = pr.get("sort")
  1223→        self._providers_only = pr.get("only")
  1224→        self._providers_ignore = pr.get("ignore")
  1225→        self._providers_order = pr.get("order")
  1226→        self._provider_require_params = pr.get("require_parameters", False)
  1227→        self._provider_data_collection = pr.get("data_collection")
  1228→        
  1229→        # Fallback model config — tried when primary provider fails after retries
  1230→        fb = CLI_CONFIG.get("fallback_model") or {}
  1231→        self._fallback_model = fb if fb.get("provider") and fb.get("model") else None
  1232→
  1233→        # Agent will be initialized on first use
  1234→        self.agent: Optional[AIAgent] = None
  1235→        self._app = None  # prompt_toolkit Application (set in run())

  1237→        # Conversation state

  1242→        self._session_db = None
  1243→        try:

  1249→        # Deferred title: stored in memory until the session is created in the DB

  1275→    def _invalidate(self, min_interval: float = 0.25) -> None:
  1276→        """Throttled UI repaint — prevents terminal blinking on slow/SSH connections."""
  1277→        import time as _time
  1278→        now = _time.monotonic()
  1279→        if hasattr(self, "_app") and self._app and (now - self._last_invalidate) >= min_interval:
  1280→            self._last_invalidate = now
  1281→            self._app.invalidate()

  1295→        and let the API be the judge.  No allowlists, no slug checks.
  1296→

1→"""Shared ANSI color utilities for Hermes CLI modules."""
     2→
     3→import sys
     4→
     5→
     6→class Colors:
     7→    RESET = "\033[0m"
     8→    BOLD = "\033[1m"
     9→    DIM = "\033[2m"
    10→    RED = "\033[31m"
    11→    GREEN = "\033[32m"
    12→    YELLOW = "\033[33m"
    13→    BLUE = "\033[34m"
    14→    MAGENTA = "\033[35m"
    15→    CYAN = "\033[36m"
    16→
    17→
    18→def color(text: str, *codes) -> str:
    19→    """Apply color codes to text (only when output is a TTY)."""
    20→    if not sys.stdout.isatty():
    21→        return text
    22→    return "".join(codes) + text + Colors.RESET
    23→

   401→def _resolve_last_cli_session() -> Optional[str]:
   402→    """Look up the most recent CLI session ID from SQLite. Returns None if unavailable."""
   403→    try:
   404→        from hermes_state import SessionDB
   405→        db = SessionDB()
   406→        sessions = db.search_sessions(source="cli", limit=1)
   407→        db.close()
   408→        if sessions:
   409→            return sessions[0]["id"]
   410→    except Exception:
   411→        pass
   412→    return None
   413→
   414→
   415→def _resolve_session_by_name_or_id(name_or_id: str) -> Optional[str]:
   416→    """Resolve a session name (title) or ID to a session ID.
   417→
   418→    - If it looks like a session ID (contains underscore + hex), try direct lookup first.
   419→    - Otherwise, treat it as a title and use resolve_session_by_title (auto-latest).
   420→    - Falls back to the other method if the first doesn't match.
   421→    """
   422→    try:
   423→        from hermes_state import SessionDB
   424→        db = SessionDB()
   425→
   426→        # Try as exact session ID first
   427→        session = db.get_session(name_or_id)
   428→        if session:
   429→            db.close()
   430→            return session["id"]
   431→
   432→        # Try as title (with auto-latest for lineage)
   433→        session_id = db.resolve_session_by_title(name_or_id)
   434→        db.close()
   435→        return session_id
   436→    except Exception:
   437→        pass
   438→    return None
   439→
   440→
   441→def cmd_chat(args):
   442→    """Run interactive chat CLI."""
   443→    # Resolve --continue into --resume with the latest CLI session or by name
   444→    continue_val = getattr(args, "continue_last", None)
   445→    if continue_val and not getattr(args, "resume", None):
   446→        if isinstance(continue_val, str):
   447→            # -c "session name" — resolve by title or ID

78→def _has_any_provider_configured() -> bool:
    79→    """Check if at least one inference provider is usable."""
    80→    from hermes_cli.config import get_env_path, get_hermes_home
    81→    from hermes_cli.auth import get_auth_status
    82→
    83→    # Check env vars (may be set by .env or shell).
    84→    # OPENAI_BASE_URL alone counts — local models (vLLM, llama.cpp, etc.)
    85→    # often don't require an API key.
    86→    from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY
    87→
    88→    # Collect all provider env vars
    89→    provider_env_vars = {"OPENROUTER_API_KEY", "OPENAI_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_TOKEN", "OPENAI_BASE_URL"}
    90→    for pconfig in PROVIDER_REGISTRY.values():
    91→        if pconfig.auth_type == "api_key":
    92→            provider_env_vars.update(pconfig.api_key_env_vars)
    93→    if any(os.getenv(v) for v in provider_env_vars):
    94→        return True
    95→
    96→    # Check .env file for keys
    97→    env_file = get_env_path()
    98→    if env_file.exists():
    99→        try:
   100→            for line in env_file.read_text().splitlines():
   101→                line = line.strip()
   102→                if line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
   103→                    continue
   104→                key, _, val = line.partition("=")
   105→                val = val.strip().strip("'\"")
   106→                if key.strip() in provider_env_vars and val:
   107→                    return True
   108→        except Exception:
   109→            pass
   110→
   111→    # Check for Nous Portal OAuth credentials
   112→    auth_file = get_hermes_home() / "auth.json"
   113→    if auth_file.exists():
   114→        try:
   115→            import json
   116→            auth = json.loads(auth_file.read_text())
   117→            active = auth.get("active_provider")
   118→            if active:
   119→                status = get_auth_status(active)
   120→                if status.get("logged_in"):
   121→                    return True
   122→        except Exception:
   123→            pass
   124→
   125→    return False
   126→
   127→

Perfect! Now let me create a comprehensive summary of my findings:

## Summary of Terminal Background Detection and Capability Code Search

Based on my thorough search of the codebase, here are my findings:

### 1. **Existing Terminal Detection Code**

#### Current Implementation in `/hermes_cli/colors.py`
- Simple `sys.stdout.isatty()` check to determine if output is a TTY
- Basic TTY detection but **no background color detection**

#### Display Module (`/agent/display.py`)
- Uses prompt_toolkit's `patch_stdout` for output redirection
- Has spinner animation with TTY output handling
- Contains rate-limiting logic for terminal output (line 254-258) to handle prompt_toolkit's queuing

### 2. **Prompt_toolkit Integration** 

#### Key Files Using prompt_toolkit:
- **`/cli.py`** (lines 36-49):
  - Imports: `Application`, `patch_stdout`, `Layout`, `HSplit`, `Window`, etc.
  - Uses `Application` for the interactive TUI
  - Uses `patch_stdout` context manager for output integration
  - No color depth or theme detection in place

- **`/hermes_cli/commands.py`**:
  - Imports `Completer` and `Completion` for command autocomplete
  - No background detection

### 3. **Color/Theme Detection System**

#### Skin Engine (`/hermes_cli/skin_engine.py`) - Lines 1-631
- **Comprehensive theming system** with multiple built-in skins:
  - `default` - Gold/kawaii theme
  - `ares` - Crimson/bronze war-god theme
  - `mono` - Monochrome grayscale
  - `slate` - Cool blue developer theme
  - `poseidon` - Ocean blue theme
  - `sisyphus` - Austere grayscale
  - `charizard` - Volcanic/ember theme

- **User-configurable skins** from `~/.hermes/skins/*.yaml`
- **Active skin loaded from CLI config** (`display.skin` in config.yaml) via `init_skin_from_config()` (line 620)
- **No automatic light/dark mode detection** - skins are manually selected

### 4. **Terminal Capability Detection - NOT IMPLEMENTED**

#### What's Missing:
- **No OSC (Operating System Command) detection** - searched for "OSC" and "osc" patterns, found none
- **No terminal capability checking** beyond basic TTY detection
- **No environment variable checks** for:
  - `TERM` environment variable
  - `COLORTERM` (truecolor support)
  - `NO_COLOR` standard
  - `FORCE_COLOR`
  - Terminal background detection sequences

### 5. **Relevant Code Locations**

| File | Path | Purpose |
|------|------|---------|
| **cli.py** | Lines 36-49 | prompt_toolkit imports and setup |
| **agent/display.py** | Lines 565-575 | OSC 8 terminal hyperlink implementation (only hyperlinks, not background detection) |
| **hermes_cli/skin_engine.py** | Full file | Complete theming/skinning system |
| **hermes_cli/colors.py** | Full file | Basic ANSI color utilities with TTY check |
| **hermes_cli/banner.py** | Lines 43-59 | Skin-aware color loading functions |

### 6. **Terminal Hyperlink Support Found**

In `/agent/display.py` (lines 565-575), there IS a function `_osc8_link()` that creates OSC 8 terminal hyperlinks:
```python
def _osc8_link(url: str, text: str) -> str:
    """OSC 8 terminal hyperlink (clickable in iTerm2, Ghostty, WezTerm, etc.)."""
    return f"\033]8;;{url}\033\\{text}\033]8;;\033\\"
```

This shows the codebase is aware of OSC sequences but only uses them for hyperlinks, not for background detection.

### 7. **Current Color Architecture**

- **Rich Console** used for banner/panel rendering
- **Manual skin selection** via `/skin` command or config file
- **No automatic detection** of terminal theme preference
- **No 24-bit color depth detection**
- **No background brightness detection**

### Conclusion

The codebase has **no existing terminal background detection code**. There are no utilities to query terminal background colors via OSC sequences (like `OSC 10` for foreground or `OSC 11` for background). The theming system is purely manual—users select a skin explicitly, and the system doesn't attempt to auto-detect whether the terminal has a light or dark background.

## Onboarding/First-Run Setup Flow Summary

### 1. **Where the Onboarding Sequence Happens**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/setup.py`  
**Main Function:** `run_setup_wizard(args)` (lines 2286-2470)

The setup wizard is invoked when:
- User runs `hermes setup` command
- First-time user runs `hermes chat` but no provider is configured (detected by `_has_any_provider_configured()` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py` lines 78-125)

---

### 2. **What Questions It Currently Asks**

The setup wizard walks through 5 modular sections (defined in `SETUP_SECTIONS` list, lines 2277-2283):

#### **Section 1: Model & Provider** (`setup_model_provider`, lines 634-1343)
- Choose inference provider from:
  - Nous Portal (OAuth)
  - OpenAI Codex (OAuth)
  - OpenRouter API key
  - Custom OpenAI-compatible endpoint
  - Z.AI / GLM (Zhipu AI)
  - Kimi / Moonshot
  - MiniMax (global)
  - MiniMax China
  - Anthropic (Claude)
  - Keep current (if provider exists)
- Selects a model from the chosen provider

#### **Section 2: Terminal Backend** (`setup_terminal_backend`, lines 1344-1650)
- Where shell commands/code run:
  - Local (default)
  - Docker (asks for image, container resources)
  - Modal (serverless)
  - SSH (asks for host, user, port)
  - Daytona (persistent cloud environment)
  - Singularity/Apptainer (on Linux)
- For Local: working directory for messaging, sudo password
- For Docker/containers: image, CPU, memory, disk limits

#### **Section 3: Agent Settings** (`setup_agent_settings`, lines 1650-1815)
- Maximum iterations (tool-calling turns)
- Tool progress display mode (off/new/all/verbose)
- Context compression settings (threshold: 0.5-0.95)
- Session reset policy (inactivity, daily, both, or never)

#### **Section 4: Messaging Platforms (Gateway)** (`setup_gateway`, lines 1818-2149)
- **Telegram:** bot token, allowed user IDs, home channel ID
- **Discord:** bot token, allowed user IDs, server ID, home channel
- Other platforms: Signal, Email, WhatsApp, HomeAssistant (optional setup)

#### **Section 5: Tools** (`setup_tools`, lines 2150-2162)
- Delegates to `tools_command()` in `hermes_cli/tools_config.py`
- Configures tool providers and API keys (TTS, web search, image generation, etc.)

---

### 3. **How the Config is Saved After Onboarding**

**Function:** `save_config(config)` in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/config.py` (lines 943-966)

**Process:**
1. After each setup section, `save_config(config)` is called (line 2322, 2469)
2. Saves to **`~/.hermes/config.yaml`** (obtained via `get_config_path()`, line 44)
3. Uses atomic YAML write via `atomic_yaml_write()` (line 961) to prevent corruption
4. File permissions secured with `_secure_file(config_path)` (line 966)
5. API keys and secrets stored separately in **`~/.hermes/.env`** via `save_env_value()` function

**Directory structure created:**
- `~/.hermes/` - main Hermes home (line 73)
- `~/.hermes/cron/` - cron jobs
- `~/.hermes/sessions/` - session data
- `~/.hermes/logs/` - logs
- `~/.hermes/memories/` - persistent memories

---

### 4. **Where the Config is Loaded at Startup**

**Primary Flow (CLI):**
1. **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py`
   - `cmd_chat()` function (line 441) calls `_has_any_provider_configured()` (lines 78-125)
   - This checks for provider config before launching

2. **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py`
   - `load_cli_config()` function (lines 142-254) loads configuration:
     - Checks user config first: `~/.hermes/config.yaml`
     - Falls back to project config: `cli-config.yaml` (in repo root)
     - Deep merges user config over defaults
     - Returns combined config dict

3. **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/config.py`
   - `load_config()` (lines 857-880): Loads YAML from `~/.hermes/config.yaml`
   - `load_env()` (lines 969-985): Loads environment variables from `~/.hermes/.env`
   - Deep merges with `DEFAULT_CONFIG` (lines 86-230)
   - Normalizes `max_turns` field migration

**Gateway Flow (Messaging):**
1. **File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/gateway/run.py` (lines 31-80)
   - Loads `.env` from `~/.hermes/.env` using `dotenv` (lines 35-43)
   - Loads `config.yaml` from `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (lines 47-80)
   - Bridges config values into environment variables for downstream use

**Config Search Priority:**
1. Shell environment variables (highest priority)
2. `~/.hermes/.env` file
3. `~/.hermes/config.yaml` file
4. Project defaults

---

    38→        "/reasoning": "Manage reasoning effort and display (usage: /reasoning [level|show|hide])",
    39→        "/skin": "Show or change the display skin/theme",
    40→    },
    41→    "Tools & Skills": {
    42→        "/tools": "List available tools",
    43→        "/toolsets": "List available toolsets",
    44→        "/skills": "Search, install, inspect, or manage skills from online registries",
    45→        "/cron": "Manage scheduled tasks (list, add, remove)",
    46→        "/reload-mcp": "Reload MCP servers from config.yaml",
    47→    },
    48→    "Info": {
    49→        "/help": "Show this help message",
    50→        "/usage": "Show token usage for the current session",
    51→        "/insights": "Show usage insights and analytics (last 30 days)",
    52→        "/platforms": "Show gateway/messaging platform status",
    53→        "/paste": "Check clipboard for an image and attach it",
    54→    },
    55→    "Exit": {
    56→        "/quit": "Exit the CLI (also: /exit, /q)",
    57→    },
    58→}
    59→
    60→# Flat dict for backwards compatibility and autocomplete
    61→COMMANDS = {}
    62→for category_commands in COMMANDS_BY_CATEGORY.values():
    63→    COMMANDS.update(category_commands)
    64→
    65→
    66→class SlashCommandCompleter(Completer):
    67→    """Autocomplete for built-in slash commands and optional skill commands."""
    68→
    69→    def __init__(
    70→        self,
    71→        skill_commands_provider: Callable[[], Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]] | None = None,
    72→    ) -> None:
    73→        self._skill_commands_provider = skill_commands_provider
    74→
    75→    def _iter_skill_commands(self) -> Mapping[str, dict[str, Any]]:
    76→        if self._skill_commands_provider is None:
    77→            return {}
    78→        try:
    79→            return self._skill_commands_provider() or {}
    80→        except Exception:
    81→            return {}
    82→
    83→    @staticmethod
    84→    def _completion_text(cmd_name: str, word: str) -> str:
    85→        """Return replacement text for a completion.
    86→
    87→        When the user has already typed the full command exactly (``/help``),
    88→        returning ``help`` would be a no-op and prompt_toolkit suppresses the
    89→        menu. Appending a trailing space keeps the dropdown visible and makes
    90→        backspacing retrigger it naturally.
    91→        """
    92→        return f"{cmd_name} " if cmd_name == word else cmd_name
    93→
    94→    def get_completions(self, document, complete_event):
    95→        text = document.text_before_cursor
    96→        if not text.startswith("/"):
    97→            return
    98→
    99→        word = text[1:]
   100→
   101→        for cmd, desc in COMMANDS.items():
   102→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   103→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   104→                yield Completion(
   105→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   106→                    start_position=-len(word),
   107→                    display=cmd,
   108→                    display_meta=desc,
   109→                )
   110→
   111→        for cmd, info in self._iter_skill_commands().items():
   112→            cmd_name = cmd[1:]
   113→            if cmd_name.startswith(word):
   114→                description = str(info.get("description", "Skill command"))
   115→                short_desc = description[:50] + ("..." if len(description) > 50 else "")
   116→                yield Completion(
   117→                    self._completion_text(cmd_name, word),
   118→                    start_position=-len(word),
   119→                    display=cmd,
   120→                    display_meta=f"⚡ {short_desc}",
   121→                )

  3119→    def _toggle_verbose(self):
  3120→        """Cycle tool progress mode: off → new → all → verbose → off."""
  3121→        cycle = ["off", "new", "all", "verbose"]
  3122→        try:
  3123→            idx = cycle.index(self.tool_progress_mode)
  3124→        except ValueError:
  3125→            idx = 2  # default to "all"
  3126→        self.tool_progress_mode = cycle[(idx + 1) % len(cycle)]
  3127→        self.verbose = self.tool_progress_mode == "verbose"
  3128→
  3129→        if self.agent:
  3130→            self.agent.verbose_logging = self.verbose
  3131→            self.agent.quiet_mode = not self.verbose

2343→    def _handle_prompt_command(self, cmd: str):
  2344→        """Handle the /prompt command to view or set system prompt."""
  2345→        parts = cmd.split(maxsplit=1)
  2346→        
  2347→        if len(parts) > 1:
  2348→            # Set new prompt
  2349→            new_prompt = parts[1].strip()
  2350→            
  2351→            if new_prompt.lower() == "clear":
  2352→                self.system_prompt = ""
  2353→                self.agent = None  # Force re-init
  2354→                if save_config_value("agent.system_prompt", ""):
  2355→                    print("(^_^)b System prompt cleared (saved to config)")
  2356→                else:
  2357→                    print("(^_^) System prompt cleared (session only)")
  2358→            else:
  2359→                self.system_prompt = new_prompt
  2360→                self.agent = None  # Force re-init
  2361→                if save_config_value("agent.system_prompt", new_prompt):
  2362→                    print(f"(^_^)b System prompt set (saved to config)")
  2363→                else:
  2364→                    print(f"(^_^) System prompt set (session only)")
  2365→                print(f"  \"{new_prompt[:60]}{'...' if len(new_prompt) > 60 else ''}\"")
  2366→        else:
  2367→            # Show current prompt
  2368→            print()
  2369→            print("+" + "-" * 50 + "+")
  2370→            print("|" + " " * 15 + "(^_^) System Prompt" + " " * 15 + "|")
  2371→            print("+" + "-" * 50 + "+")
  2372→            print()
  2373→            if self.system_prompt:
  2374→                # Word wrap the prompt for display
  2375→                words = self.system_prompt.split()
  2376→                lines = []
  2377→                current_line = ""
  2378→                for word in words:
  2379→                    if len(current_line) + len(word) + 1 <= 50:
  2380→                        current_line += (" " if current_line else "") + word
  2381→                    else:
  2382→                        lines.append(current_line)
  2383→                        current_line = word
  2384→                if current_line:
  2385→                    lines.append(current_line)
  2386→                for line in lines:
  2387→                    print(f"  {line}")
  2388→            else:
  2389→                print("  (no custom prompt set - using default)")
  2390→            print()
  2391→            print("  Usage:")
  2392→            print("    /prompt <text>  - Set a custom system prompt")
  2393→            print("    /prompt clear   - Remove custom prompt")
  2394→            print("    /personality    - Use a predefined personality")
  2395→            print()
  2396→    
  2397→
  2398→    @staticmethod
  2399→    def _resolve_personality_prompt(value) -> str:
  2400→        """Accept string or dict personality value; return system prompt string."""
  2401→        if isinstance(value, dict):
  2402→            parts = [value.get("system_prompt", "")]
  2403→            if value.get("tone"):
  2404→                parts.append(f'Tone: {value["tone"]}' )
  2405→            if value.get("style"):
  2406→                parts.append(f'Style: {value["style"]}' )
  2407→            return "\n".join(p for p in parts if p)
  2408→        return str(value)
  2409→
  2410→    def _handle_personality_command(self, cmd: str):
  2411→        """Handle the /personality command to set predefined personalities."""
  2412→        parts = cmd.split(maxsplit=1)
  2413→        
  2414→        if len(parts) > 1:
  2415→            # Set personality
  2416→            personality_name = parts[1].strip().lower()
  2417→            
  2418→            if personality_name in ("none", "default", "neutral"):
  2419→                self.system_prompt = ""
  2420→                self.agent = None  # Force re-init
  2421→                if save_config_value("agent.system_prompt", ""):
  2422→                    print("(^_^)b Personality cleared (saved to config)")

1024→def save_config_value(key_path: str, value: any) -> bool:
  1025→    """
  1026→    Save a value to the active config file at the specified key path.
  1027→    
  1028→    Respects the same lookup order as load_cli_config():
  1029→    1. ~/.hermes/config.yaml (user config - preferred, used if it exists)
  1030→    2. ./cli-config.yaml (project config - fallback)
  1031→    
  1032→    Args:
  1033→        key_path: Dot-separated path like "agent.system_prompt"
  1034→        value: Value to save
  1035→    
  1036→    Returns:
  1037→        True if successful, False otherwise
  1038→    """
  1039→    # Use the same precedence as load_cli_config: user config first, then project config
  1040→    user_config_path = Path.home() / '.hermes' / 'config.yaml'
  1041→    project_config_path = Path(__file__).parent / 'cli-config.yaml'
  1042→    config_path = user_config_path if user_config_path.exists() else project_config_path
  1043→    
  1044→    try:
  1045→        # Ensure parent directory exists (for ~/.hermes/config.yaml on first use)
  1046→        config_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  1047→        
  1048→        # Load existing config
  1049→        if config_path.exists():
  1050→            with open(config_path, 'r') as f:
  1051→                config = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1052→        else:
  1053→            config = {}
  1054→        
  1055→        # Navigate to the key and set value
  1056→        keys = key_path.split('.')
  1057→        current = config
  1058→        for key in keys[:-1]:
  1059→            if key not in current or not isinstance(current[key], dict):
  1060→                current[key] = {}
  1061→            current = current[key]
  1062→        current[keys[-1]] = value
  1063→        
  1064→        # Save back
  1065→        with open(config_path, 'w') as f:
  1066→            yaml.dump(config, f, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)
  1067→        
  1068→        # Enforce owner-only permissions on config files (contain API keys)
  1069→        try:
  1070→            os.chmod(config_path, 0o600)
  1071→        except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
  1072→            pass
  1073→        
  1074→        return True
  1075→    except Exception as e:
  1076→        logger.error("Failed to save config: %s", e)
  1077→        return False
  1078→
  1079→
  1080→# ============================================================================
  1081→# HermesCLI Class
  1082→# ============================================================================
  1083→
```

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me create a summary of my findings:

## Summary of Setup Flow Analysis

Based on my review of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/setup.py` and `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py`, here are the answers to your questions:

### 1. **`_run_quick_setup` Function (lines 2504-2668)**
The quick setup covers **only missing items**. It explicitly does NOT run full sections like the normal setup wizard. Instead:
- Checks for missing required environment variables and prompts for them
- Checks for missing optional variables grouped by category (tool API keys, messaging platforms)
- Prompts to configure only the selected missing tools and messaging platforms
- Adds any new missing config fields with defaults
- **Does NOT ask about agent settings** (iterations, compression, session reset)
- **Does NOT ask about terminal backend**
- **Does NOT ask about model/provider** (assumes already configured, since this is "quick setup" for returning users)

In other words, `_run_quick_setup` skips agent settings entirely and focuses only on API keys and environment variables.

### 2. **First-Time Setup Flow (lines 2453-2501 in `run_setup_wizard`)**
When `is_existing` is False (no provider configured), the wizard runs ALL sections in this order:

1. **Model & Provider** (line 2485) - `setup_model_provider(config)`
2. **Terminal Backend** (line 2488) - `setup_terminal_backend(config)`
3. **Agent Settings** (line 2491) - `setup_agent_settings(config)` ← **Agent settings IS included**
4. **Messaging Platforms** (line 2494) - `setup_gateway(config)`
5. **Tools** (line 2497) - `setup_tools(config, first_install=not is_existing)`

Then saves config and shows summary.

### 3. **Display/Theme Preferences in First-Run**
**YES**, display/theme preferences ARE already part of first-run setup! They're handled in the **Agent Settings** section (which IS run on first setup):

Within `setup_agent_settings` (lines 1650-1810):
- **Tool Progress Display** (lines 1674-1692): Asks for mode (off/new/all/verbose) → stored in `config["display"]["tool_progress"]`
- **Terminal Theme Mode** (lines 1694-1723): Asks for theme preference (auto/dark/light) → stored in `config["display"]["theme_mode"]`

The theme detection logic is at lines 1709-1719, which includes auto-detection via `detect_terminal_background()`.

### 4. **First Launch Behavior in `main.py` (lines 473-489)**
On first launch:
- The `_has_any_provider_configured()` check (line 474) prevents the agent from running without credentials
- **Users are NOT forced through setup** — they get a prompt:
  ```
  "It looks like Hermes isn't configured yet -- no API keys or providers found.
   Run:  hermes setup
   Run setup now? [Y/n]"
  ```
- Users can **skip setup** by answering "n" (line 481-483), though the CLI then exits with `sys.exit(1)`
- If they answer "y" or press Enter, `cmd_setup(args)` is called (line 485)

**So setup is not mandatory on first launch** — users can technically skip it, but the CLI won't work without it, so they'd need to run `hermes setup` manually later.

---

### File Locations:
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/setup.py` (main setup logic)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py` (entry point and first-run guard)

  1844→def _update_via_zip(args):
  1845→    """Update Hermes Agent by downloading a ZIP archive.
  1846→    
  1847→    Used on Windows when git file I/O is broken (antivirus, NTFS filter 
  1848→    drivers causing 'Invalid argument' errors on file creation).
  1849→    """
  1850→    import shutil
  1851→    import tempfile
  1852→    import zipfile
  1853→    from urllib.request import urlretrieve
  1854→    
  1855→    branch = "main"
  1856→    zip_url = f"https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/archive/refs/heads/{branch}.zip"
  1857→    
  1858→    print("→ Downloading latest version...")
  1859→    try:
  1860→        tmp_dir = tempfile.mkdtemp(prefix="hermes-update-")
  1861→        zip_path = os.path.join(tmp_dir, f"hermes-agent-{branch}.zip")
  1862→        urlretrieve(zip_url, zip_path)
  1863→        
  1864→        print("→ Extracting...")
  1865→        with zipfile.ZipFile(zip_path, 'r') as zf:
  1866→            zf.extractall(tmp_dir)
  1867→        
  1868→        # GitHub ZIPs extract to hermes-agent-<branch>/
  1869→        extracted = os.path.join(tmp_dir, f"hermes-agent-{branch}")
  1870→        if not os.path.isdir(extracted):
  1871→            # Try to find it
  1872→            for d in os.listdir(tmp_dir):
  1873→                candidate = os.path.join(tmp_dir, d)
  1874→                if os.path.isdir(candidate) and d != "__MACOSX":
  1875→                    extracted = candidate
  1876→                    break
  1877→        
  1878→        # Copy updated files over existing installation, preserving venv/node_modules/.git
  1879→        preserve = {'venv', 'node_modules', '.git', '__pycache__', '.env'}
  1880→        update_count = 0
  1881→        for item in os.listdir(extracted):
  1882→            if item in preserve:
  1883→                continue
  1884→            src = os.path.join(extracted, item)
  1885→            dst = os.path.join(str(PROJECT_ROOT), item)
  1886→            if os.path.isdir(src):
  1887→                if os.path.exists(dst):
  1888→                    shutil.rmtree(dst)
  1889→                shutil.copytree(src, dst)
  1890→            else:
  1891→                shutil.copy2(src, dst)
  1892→            update_count += 1
  1893→        
  1894→        print(f"✓ Updated {update_count} items from ZIP")
  1895→        
  1896→        # Cleanup
  1897→        shutil.rmtree(tmp_dir, ignore_errors=True)
  1898→        
  1899→    except Exception as e:
  1900→        print(f"✗ ZIP update failed: {e}")
  1901→        sys.exit(1)
  1902→    
  1903→    # Reinstall Python dependencies
  1904→    print("→ Updating Python dependencies...")
  1905→    import subprocess
  1906→    uv_bin = shutil.which("uv")
  1907→    if uv_bin:
  1908→        subprocess.run(
  1909→            [uv_bin, "pip", "install", "-e", ".", "--quiet"],
  1910→            cwd=PROJECT_ROOT, check=True,
  1911→            env={**os.environ, "VIRTUAL_ENV": str(PROJECT_ROOT / "venv")}
  1912→        )
  1913→    else:
  1914→        venv_pip = PROJECT_ROOT / "venv" / ("Scripts" if sys.platform == "win32" else "bin") / "pip"
  1915→        if venv_pip.exists():
  1916→            subprocess.run([str(venv_pip), "install", "-e", ".", "--quiet"], cwd=PROJECT_ROOT, check=True)
  1917→    
  1918→    # Sync skills
  1919→    try:
  1920→        from tools.skills_sync import sync_skills
  1921→        print("→ Syncing bundled skills...")
  1922→        result = sync_skills(quiet=True)
  1923→        if result["copied"]:
  1924→            print(f"  + {len(result['copied'])} new: {', '.join(result['copied'])}")
  1925→        if result.get("updated"):
  1926→            print(f"  ↑ {len(result['updated'])} updated: {', '.join(result['updated'])}")
  1927→        if result.get("user_modified"):
  1928→            print(f"  ~ {len(result['user_modified'])} user-modified (kept)")
  1929→        if result.get("cleaned"):
  1930→            print(f"  − {len(result['cleaned'])} removed from manifest")
  1931→        if not result["copied"] and not result.get("updated"):
  1932→            print("  ✓ Skills are up to date")
  1933→    except Exception:
  1934→        pass
  1935→    
  1936→    print()
  1937→    print("✓ Update complete!")
  1938→
  1939→
  1940→def cmd_update(args):
  1941→    """Update Hermes Agent to the latest version."""
  1942→    import subprocess
  1943→    import shutil
  1944→    
  1945→    print("⚕ Updating Hermes Agent...")
  1946→    print()
  1947→    
  1948→    # Try git-based update first, fall back to ZIP download on Windows
  1949→    # when git file I/O is broken (antivirus, NTFS filter drivers, etc.)
  1950→    use_zip_update = False
  1951→    git_dir = PROJECT_ROOT / '.git'

  1953→    if not git_dir.exists():
  1954→        if sys.platform == "win32":
  1955→            use_zip_update = True
  1956→        else:
  1957→            print("✗ Not a git repository. Please reinstall:")
  1958→            print("  curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/main/scripts/install.sh | bash")
  1959→            sys.exit(1)
  1960→    
  1961→    # On Windows, git can fail with "unable to write loose object file: Invalid argument"
  1962→    # due to filesystem atomicity issues. Set the recommended workaround.
  1963→    if sys.platform == "win32" and git_dir.exists():
  1964→        subprocess.run(
  1965→            ["git", "-c", "windows.appendAtomically=false", "config", "windows.appendAtomically", "false"],

1231→def edit_config():

1260→def set_config_value(key: str, value: str):
  1261→    """Set a configuration value."""
  1262→    # Check if it's an API key (goes to .env)
  1263→    api_keys = [
  1264→        'OPENROUTER_API_KEY', 'OPENAI_API_KEY', 'ANTHROPIC_API_KEY', 'VOICE_TOOLS_OPENAI_KEY',
  1265→        'FIRECRAWL_API_KEY', 'FIRECRAWL_API_URL', 'BROWSERBASE_API_KEY', 'BROWSERBASE_PROJECT_ID',
  1266→        'FAL_KEY', 'TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN', 'DISCORD_BOT_TOKEN',
  1267→        'TERMINAL_SSH_HOST', 'TERMINAL_SSH_USER', 'TERMINAL_SSH_KEY',
  1268→        'SUDO_PASSWORD', 'SLACK_BOT_TOKEN', 'SLACK_APP_TOKEN',
  1269→        'GITHUB_TOKEN', 'HONCHO_API_KEY', 'WANDB_API_KEY',
  1270→        'TINKER_API_KEY',
  1271→    ]
  1272→    
  1273→    if key.upper() in api_keys or key.upper().endswith('_API_KEY') or key.upper().endswith('_TOKEN') or key.upper().startswith('TERMINAL_SSH'):
  1274→        save_env_value(key.upper(), value)
  1275→        print(f"✓ Set {key} in {get_env_path()}")
  1276→        return
  1277→    
  1278→    # Otherwise it goes to config.yaml
  1279→    # Read the raw user config (not merged with defaults) to avoid
  1280→    # dumping all default values back to the file
  1281→    config_path = get_config_path()
  1282→    user_config = {}
  1283→    if config_path.exists():
  1284→        try:
  1285→            with open(config_path, encoding="utf-8") as f:
  1286→                user_config = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1287→        except Exception:
  1288→            user_config = {}
  1289→    
  1290→    # Handle nested keys (e.g., "tts.provider")
  1291→    parts = key.split('.')
  1292→    current = user_config
  1293→    
  1294→    for part in parts[:-1]:
  1295→        if part not in current or not isinstance(current.get(part), dict):
  1296→            current[part] = {}
  1297→        current = current[part]
  1298→    
  1299→    # Convert value to appropriate type
  1300→    if value.lower() in ('true', 'yes', 'on'):
  1301→        value = True
  1302→    elif value.lower() in ('false', 'no', 'off'):
  1303→        value = False
  1304→    elif value.isdigit():
  1305→        value = int(value)
  1306→    elif value.replace('.', '', 1).isdigit():
  1307→        value = float(value)
  1308→    
  1309→    current[parts[-1]] = value
  1310→    
  1311→    # Write only user config back (not the full merged defaults)
  1312→    ensure_hermes_home()
  1313→    with open(config_path, 'w', encoding="utf-8") as f:
  1314→        yaml.dump(user_config, f, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)
  1315→    
  1316→    # Keep .env in sync for keys that terminal_tool reads directly from env vars.
  1317→    # config.yaml is authoritative, but terminal_tool only reads TERMINAL_ENV etc.
  1318→    _config_to_env_sync = {
  1319→        "terminal.backend": "TERMINAL_ENV",
  1320→        "terminal.docker_image": "TERMINAL_DOCKER_IMAGE",
  1321→        "terminal.singularity_image": "TERMINAL_SINGULARITY_IMAGE",

  1323→        "terminal.daytona_image": "TERMINAL_DAYTONA_IMAGE",
  1324→        "terminal.cwd": "TERMINAL_CWD",
  1325→        "terminal.timeout": "TERMINAL_TIMEOUT",
  1326→        "terminal.sandbox_dir": "TERMINAL_SANDBOX_DIR",
  1327→    }
  1328→    if key in _config_to_env_sync:
  1329→        save_env_value(_config_to_env_sync[key], str(value))
  1330→
  1331→    print(f"✓ Set {key} = {value} in {config_path}")

1→"""Hermes CLI skin/theme engine.
     2→
     3→A data-driven skin system that lets users customize the CLI's visual appearance.
     4→Skins are defined as YAML files in ~/.hermes/skins/ or as built-in presets.
     5→No code changes are needed to add a new skin.
     6→
     7→SKIN YAML SCHEMA
     8→================
     9→
    10→All fields are optional. Missing values inherit from the ``default`` skin.
    11→
    12→.. code-block:: yaml
    13→
    14→    # Required: skin identity
    15→    name: mytheme                         # Unique skin name (lowercase, hyphens ok)
    16→    description: Short description        # Shown in /skin listing
    17→
    18→    # Colors: hex values for Rich markup (banner, UI, response box)
    19→    # These are the default (dark terminal) colors.
    20→    colors:
    21→      banner_border: "#CD7F32"            # Panel border color
    22→      banner_title: "#FFD700"             # Panel title text color
    23→      banner_accent: "#FFBF00"            # Section headers (Available Tools, etc.)
    24→      banner_dim: "#B8860B"               # Dim/muted text (separators, labels)
    25→      banner_text: "#FFF8DC"              # Body text (tool names, skill names)
    26→      ui_accent: "#FFBF00"               # General UI accent
    27→      ui_label: "#4dd0e1"                # UI labels
    28→      ui_ok: "#4caf50"                   # Success indicators
    29→      ui_error: "#ef5350"                # Error indicators
    30→      ui_warn: "#ffa726"                 # Warning indicators
    31→      prompt: "#FFF8DC"                  # Prompt text color
    32→      input_rule: "#CD7F32"              # Input area horizontal rule
    33→      response_border: "#FFD700"         # Response box border (ANSI)
    34→      session_label: "#DAA520"           # Session label color
    35→      session_border: "#8B8682"          # Session ID dim color
    36→
    37→    # Light-mode color overrides (optional). Only keys that need to change.
    38→    # When theme_mode is "light", these replace the corresponding colors above.
    39→    colors_light:
    40→      banner_title: "#B8860B"
    41→      prompt: "#5C4A00"
    42→
    43→    # Spinner: customize the animated spinner during API calls
    44→    spinner:
    45→      waiting_faces:                      # Faces shown while waiting for API
    46→        - "(⚔)"
    47→        - "(⛨)"
    48→      thinking_faces:                     # Faces shown during reasoning
    49→        - "(⌁)"
    50→        - "(<>)"
    51→      thinking_verbs:                     # Verbs for spinner messages
    52→        - "forging"
    53→        - "plotting"
    54→      wings:                              # Optional left/right spinner decorations
    55→        - ["⟪⚔", "⚔⟫"]                  # Each entry is [left, right] pair
    56→        - ["⟪▲", "▲⟫"]
    57→
    58→    # Branding: text strings used throughout the CLI
    59→    branding:
    60→      agent_name: "Hermes Agent"          # Banner title, status display
    61→      welcome: "Welcome message"          # Shown at CLI startup
    62→      goodbye: "Goodbye! ⚕"              # Shown on exit
    63→      response_label: " ⚕ Hermes "       # Response box header label
    64→      prompt_symbol: "❯ "                # Input prompt symbol
    65→      help_header: "(^_^)? Commands"      # /help header text
    66→
    67→    # Tool prefix: character for tool output lines (default: ┊)
    68→    tool_prefix: "┊"
    69→
    70→USAGE
    71→=====
    72→
    73→.. code-block:: python
    74→
    75→    from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin, list_skins, set_active_skin
    76→
    77→    skin = get_active_skin()
    78→    print(skin.colors["banner_title"])    # "#FFD700"
    79→    print(skin.get_branding("agent_name"))  # "Hermes Agent"
    80→
    81→    set_active_skin("ares")               # Switch to built-in ares skin
    82→    set_active_skin("mytheme")            # Switch to user skin from ~/.hermes/skins/
    83→
    84→BUILT-IN SKINS
    85→==============
    86→
    87→- ``default`` — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii (the current look)
    88→- ``ares``    — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with custom spinner wings
    89→- ``mono``    — Clean grayscale monochrome
    90→- ``slate``   — Cool blue developer-focused theme
    91→
    92→USER SKINS
    93→==========
    94→
    95→Drop a YAML file in ``~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`` following the schema above.
    96→Activate with ``/skin <name>`` in the CLI or ``display.skin: <name>`` in config.yaml.
    97→"""
    98→
    99→import logging
   100→import os
   101→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
   102→from pathlib import Path
   103→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
   104→
   105→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
   106→
   107→
   108→# =============================================================================
   109→# Skin data structure
   110→# =============================================================================
   111→
   112→@dataclass
   113→class SkinConfig:
   114→    """Complete skin configuration."""
   115→    name: str
   116→    description: str = ""
   117→    colors: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   118→    colors_light: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   119→    spinner: Dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)
   120→    branding: Dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)
   121→    tool_prefix: str = "┊"
   122→    banner_logo: str = ""    # Rich-markup ASCII art logo (replaces HERMES_AGENT_LOGO)
   123→    banner_hero: str = ""    # Rich-markup hero art (replaces HERMES_CADUCEUS)
   124→
   125→    def get_color(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   126→        """Get a color value with fallback, respecting the active theme mode.
   127→
   128→        If the active theme mode is "light" and a light-mode override exists
   129→        for this key, return it. Otherwise return the default (dark) color.
   130→        """
   131→        mode = get_theme_mode()
   132→        if mode == "light" and key in self.colors_light:
   133→            return self.colors_light[key]
   134→        return self.colors.get(key, fallback)
   135→
   136→    def get_spinner_list(self, key: str) -> List[str]:
   137→        """Get a spinner list (faces, verbs, etc.)."""
   138→        return self.spinner.get(key, [])
   139→
   140→    def get_spinner_wings(self) -> List[Tuple[str, str]]:
   141→        """Get spinner wing pairs, or empty list if none."""
   142→        raw = self.spinner.get("wings", [])
   143→        result = []
   144→        for pair in raw:
   145→            if isinstance(pair, (list, tuple)) and len(pair) == 2:
   146→                result.append((str(pair[0]), str(pair[1])))
   147→        return result
   148→
   149→    def get_branding(self, key: str, fallback: str = "") -> str:
   150→        """Get a branding value with fallback."""
   151→        return self.branding.get(key, fallback)
   152→

   576→
   577→# =============================================================================
   578→# Skin loading and management
   579→# =============================================================================
   580→
   581→_active_skin: Optional[SkinConfig] = None
   582→_active_skin_name: str = "default"
   583→_theme_mode: str = "auto"  # "auto", "light", or "dark"
   584→_resolved_theme_mode: Optional[str] = None  # Cached result of auto-detection
   585→
   586→
   587→def _skins_dir() -> Path:
   588→    """User skins directory."""
   589→    home = Path(os.getenv("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   590→    return home / "skins"
   591→
   592→
   593→def _load_skin_from_yaml(path: Path) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   594→    """Load a skin definition from a YAML file."""
   595→    try:
   596→        import yaml
   597→        with open(path, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
   598→            data = yaml.safe_load(f)
   599→        if isinstance(data, dict) and "name" in data:
   600→            return data
   601→    except Exception as e:
   602→        logger.debug("Failed to load skin from %s: %s", path, e)
   603→    return None
   604→
   605→
   606→def _build_skin_config(data: Dict[str, Any]) -> SkinConfig:
   607→    """Build a SkinConfig from a raw dict (built-in or loaded from YAML)."""
   608→    # Start with default values as base for missing keys
   609→    default = _BUILTIN_SKINS["default"]
   610→    colors = dict(default.get("colors", {}))
   611→    colors.update(data.get("colors", {}))
   612→    colors_light = dict(default.get("colors_light", {}))
   613→    colors_light.update(data.get("colors_light", {}))
   614→    spinner = dict(default.get("spinner", {}))
   615→    spinner.update(data.get("spinner", {}))
   616→    branding = dict(default.get("branding", {}))
   617→    branding.update(data.get("branding", {}))
   618→
   619→    return SkinConfig(
   620→        name=data.get("name", "unknown"),
   621→        description=data.get("description", ""),
   622→        colors=colors,
   623→        colors_light=colors_light,
   624→        spinner=spinner,
   625→        branding=branding,
   626→        tool_prefix=data.get("tool_prefix", default.get("tool_prefix", "┊")),
   627→        banner_logo=data.get("banner_logo", ""),
   628→        banner_hero=data.get("banner_hero", ""),
   629→    )
   630→
   631→
   632→def list_skins() -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
   633→    """List all available skins (built-in + user-installed).
   634→
   635→    Returns list of {"name": ..., "description": ..., "source": "builtin"|"user"}.
   636→    """
   637→    result = []
   638→    for name, data in _BUILTIN_SKINS.items():
   639→        result.append({
   640→            "name": name,
   641→            "description": data.get("description", ""),
   642→            "source": "builtin",
   643→        })
   644→
   645→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   646→    if skins_path.is_dir():
   647→        for f in sorted(skins_path.glob("*.yaml")):
   648→            data = _load_skin_from_yaml(f)
   649→            if data:
   650→                skin_name = data.get("name", f.stem)
   651→                # Skip if it shadows a built-in
   652→                if any(s["name"] == skin_name for s in result):
   653→                    continue
   654→                result.append({
   655→                    "name": skin_name,
   656→                    "description": data.get("description", ""),
   657→                    "source": "user",
   658→                })
   659→
   660→    return result
   661→
   662→
   663→def load_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   664→    """Load a skin by name. Checks user skins first, then built-in."""
   665→    # Check user skins directory
   666→    skins_path = _skins_dir()
   667→    user_file = skins_path / f"{name}.yaml"
   668→    if user_file.is_file():
   669→        data = _load_skin_from_yaml(user_file)
   670→        if data:
   671→            return _build_skin_config(data)
   672→
   673→    # Check built-in skins
   674→    if name in _BUILTIN_SKINS:
   675→        return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS[name])
   676→
   677→    # Fallback to default
   678→    logger.warning("Skin '%s' not found, using default", name)
   679→    return _build_skin_config(_BUILTIN_SKINS["default"])
   680→
   681→
   682→def get_theme_mode() -> str:
   683→    """Get the resolved theme mode ("light" or "dark").
   684→
   685→    If set to "auto", detects the terminal background on first call and caches it.
   686→    Falls back to "dark" if detection fails.
   687→    """
   688→    global _resolved_theme_mode
   689→    if _theme_mode in ("light", "dark"):
   690→        return _theme_mode
   691→    # Auto-detect
   692→    if _resolved_theme_mode is None:
   693→        try:
   694→            from hermes_cli.colors import detect_terminal_background
   695→            result = detect_terminal_background()
   696→            _resolved_theme_mode = result if result != "unknown" else "dark"
   697→        except Exception:
   698→            _resolved_theme_mode = "dark"
   699→    return _resolved_theme_mode
   700→
   701→
   702→def set_theme_mode(mode: str) -> None:
   703→    """Set the theme mode ("auto", "light", or "dark")."""
   704→    global _theme_mode, _resolved_theme_mode
   705→    if mode not in ("auto", "light", "dark"):
   706→        mode = "auto"
   707→    _theme_mode = mode
   708→    _resolved_theme_mode = None  # Reset cache so auto re-detects
   709→
   710→
   711→def get_active_skin() -> SkinConfig:
   712→    """Get the currently active skin config (cached)."""
   713→    global _active_skin
   714→    if _active_skin is None:
   715→        _active_skin = load_skin(_active_skin_name)
   716→    return _active_skin
   717→
   718→
   719→def set_active_skin(name: str) -> SkinConfig:
   720→    """Switch the active skin. Returns the new SkinConfig."""
   721→    global _active_skin, _active_skin_name
   722→    _active_skin_name = name
   723→    _active_skin = load_skin(name)
   724→    return _active_skin
   725→
   726→
   727→def get_active_skin_name() -> str:
   728→    """Get the name of the currently active skin."""
   729→    return _active_skin_name
   730→
   731→
   732→def init_skin_from_config(config: dict) -> None:
   733→    """Initialize the active skin and theme mode from CLI config at startup.
   734→
   735→    Call this once during CLI init with the loaded config dict.
   736→    """
   737→    display = config.get("display", {})
   738→    skin_name = display.get("skin", "default")
   739→    if isinstance(skin_name, str) and skin_name.strip():
   740→        set_active_skin(skin_name.strip())
   741→    else:
   742→        set_active_skin("default")
   743→
   744→    theme_mode = display.get("theme_mode", "auto")
   745→    if isinstance(theme_mode, str) and theme_mode.strip():
   746→        set_theme_mode(theme_mode.strip())
   747→    else:
   748→        set_theme_mode("auto")
   749→
```
