## Summary: Ways Users Can Change Settings Outside the Setup Wizard

Based on my exploration of the codebase, here are all the ways users can change display/theme settings and other configurations after initial setup:

### 1. **Slash Commands in Interactive CLI** (`/skin`, `/config`, `/reasoning`, `/verbose`, `/model`, `/provider`, `/prompt`, `/personality`)

#### `/skin` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 3082-3117)

- **Usage:** `/skin` or `/skin <name>`
- **Functions:**
  - Shows current skin and lists all available skins
  - Switches to a different skin by name
  - Built-in skins: `default`, `ares`, `mono`, `slate`, `poseidon`, `sisyphus`, `charizard`
  - User skins: custom YAML files in `~/.hermes/skins/`
- **How it works:**
  1. User types `/skin ares` to switch to the "ares" theme
  2. Calls `set_active_skin(new_skin)` to activate it
  3. Saves to config with `save_config_value("display.skin", new_skin)`
  4. Changes persist in `~/.hermes/config.yaml` under `display.skin`
  5. Colors update on next session start

#### `/config` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2675-2676)

- **Usage:** `/config`
- **Shows:** Current configuration including:
  - Model and API key (redacted)
  - Terminal settings (environment, working dir, timeout)
  - Agent settings (max turns, toolsets, verbose)
  - Session info (start time, config file location)

#### `/reasoning` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 3141-3199)

- **Usage:** `/reasoning`, `/reasoning <level>`, `/reasoning show|hide`
- **Functions:**
  - Show current reasoning effort level
  - Set effort level: `none`, `low`, `minimal`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`
  - Toggle display: `show`/`on` or `hide`/`off`
- **Saves to:** `display.show_reasoning` (boolean) and `agent.reasoning_effort` (string)

#### `/verbose` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2905-2907, 3119-3139)

- **Usage:** `/verbose` (cycles through modes)
- **Modes:** `off` → `new` → `all` → `verbose` → (back to off)
- **Behavior:** Cycles tool progress display mode each time called
- **Not saved** to config (session-only)

#### `/model` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2800-2880)

- **Usage:** `/model` (show available) or `/model <model-name>`
- **Functions:**
  - Shows available models and current provider
  - Switches LLM model
  - Can change provider with syntax: `provider:model` (e.g., `openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5`)
- **Saves to:** `model.default` and optionally `model.provider`

#### `/provider` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2881-2882)

- **Usage:** `/provider`
- **Shows:** Available providers and credentials status

#### `/prompt` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2883-2885, 2343-2395)

- **Usage:** `/prompt` (view), `/prompt <text>` (set), `/prompt clear` (reset)
- **Functions:** Set or view custom system prompt
- **Saves to:** `agent.system_prompt`

#### `/personality` Command
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2886-2888, 2410-2453)

- **Usage:** `/personality` (list), `/personality <name>` (set)
- **Functions:** Apply predefined personality overlays (affects system prompt)
- **Available:** Configured personalities + `none`, `default`, `neutral`
- **Saves to:** `agent.system_prompt`

---

### 2. **Command-Line Config Management** (`hermes config`)

**Files:** 
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py` (lines 2544-2569)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/config.py` (lines 1094-1472)

#### `hermes config show`
- **Display** current configuration in formatted output
- Shows API keys (redacted), model settings, display preferences, terminal settings, etc.

#### `hermes config edit`
- **Opens** `~/.hermes/config.yaml` in the user's default editor
- **$EDITOR** or **$VISUAL** environment variable used (or nano/vim/vi/code/notepad as fallback)
- Direct manual editing of all config options

#### `hermes config set <KEY> <VALUE>`
- **Set individual** configuration values
- **Examples:**
  - `hermes config set model anthropic/claude-sonnet-4`
  - `hermes config set terminal.backend docker`
  - `hermes config set display.skin ares`
  - API keys: `hermes config set OPENROUTER_API_KEY sk-or-...`
- **Handles nested keys** with dot notation: `model.default`, `terminal.backend`, `display.skin`, etc.
- **Auto-converts** values: `true`/`yes`/`on` → boolean, numbers → int/float
- **API keys/tokens** go to `~/.hermes/.env` (secure, 0600 permissions)
- **Config values** go to `~/.hermes/config.yaml`

#### `hermes config path`
- Show path to config file: `~/.hermes/config.yaml`

#### `hermes config env-path`
- Show path to secrets file: `~/.hermes/.env`

#### `hermes config check`
- Check for missing/outdated config options

#### `hermes config migrate`
- Update config with new optional fields

---

### 3. **Direct Config File Editing**

**Config Location:** `~/.hermes/config.yaml`

Users can directly edit the config file to set:

```yaml
# Display/Theme Settings
display:
  skin: ares                    # Built-in or custom skin name
  show_reasoning: true          # Show model thinking/reasoning
  personality: kawaii           # Display personality (deprecated)
  theme_mode: auto              # auto|light|dark (for skin color variants)
  bell_on_complete: false       # Play terminal bell when response done

# Model Settings
model:
  default: anthropic/claude-3-opus
  provider: openrouter

# Agent Settings
agent:
  max_turns: 25
  system_prompt: "Your custom system prompt here"
  reasoning_effort: high

# Terminal Settings
terminal:
  backend: local                # local|docker|singularity|modal|ssh
  cwd: .
  timeout: 60

# Other
toolsets: [all]
compression:
  enabled: true
  threshold: 0.5
```

**Custom User Skins:** Drop YAML files in `~/.hermes/skins/` directory
- File name becomes the skin name (e.g., `mytheme.yaml` → use `/skin mytheme`)
- Follow the schema in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/skin_engine.py` (lines 7-97)

---

### 4. **Internal Config Saving Function**

**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 1024-1077)

**Function:** `save_config_value(key_path, value)`
- Used internally by slash commands to persist settings
- Respects priority: `~/.hermes/config.yaml` (user) > `./cli-config.yaml` (project fallback)
- Creates parent directories as needed
- Sets 0600 permissions (owner-only access)
- Returns True/False for success

---

### 5. **Display Settings Summary**

**All changeable display settings:**

| Setting | Command | Config Key | Options |
|---------|---------|-----------|---------|
| **Skin/Theme** | `/skin` | `display.skin` | default, ares, mono, slate, poseidon, sisyphus, charizard, or custom |
| **Show Reasoning** | `/reasoning show\|hide` | `display.show_reasoning` | true/false |
| **Reasoning Effort** | `/reasoning <level>` | `agent.reasoning_effort` | none, low, minimal, medium, high, xhigh |
| **Tool Progress** | `/verbose` | N/A (session-only) | off, new, all, verbose |
| **System Prompt** | `/prompt <text>` | `agent.system_prompt` | any text string |
| **Personality** | `/personality <name>` | `agent.system_prompt` | predefined names or none |
| **Theme Mode** | `hermes config set` | `display.theme_mode` | auto, light, dark |
| **Bell on Complete** | `hermes config set` | `display.bell_on_complete` | true/false |
| **Model** | `/model` | `model.default` | any LLM model name |
| **Provider** | `/provider` or `/model` | `model.provider` | openrouter, anthropic, etc. |

---

### 6. **Built-in Skins Available**

All in `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/skin_engine.py` (lines 158-573):

1. **default** - Classic gold/kawaii (Hermes standard)
2. **ares** - War-god theme (crimson/bronze with custom spinner)
3. **mono** - Monochrome grayscale
4. **slate** - Cool blue developer-focused
5. **poseidon** - Ocean-god theme (deep blue/seafoam)
6. **sisyphus** - Austere grayscale with persistence theme
7. **charizard** - Volcanic theme (burnt orange/ember)

Each skin has:
- Custom colors (dark and light mode variants)
- Custom spinner faces and verbs
- Custom branding (agent name, welcome message, prompt symbol)
- Optional custom banner art

```
1→"""
     2→Interactive setup wizard for Hermes Agent.
     3→
     4→Modular wizard with independently-runnable sections:
     5→  1. Model & Provider — choose your AI provider and model
     6→  2. Terminal Backend — where your agent runs commands
     7→  3. Messaging Platforms — connect Telegram, Discord, etc.
     8→  4. Tools — configure TTS, web search, image generation, etc.
     9→  5. Agent Settings — iterations, compression, session reset
    10→
    11→Config files are stored in ~/.hermes/ for easy access.
    12→"""
    13→
    14→import importlib.util
    15→import logging
    16→import os
    17→import sys
    18→from pathlib import Path
    19→from typing import Optional, Dict, Any
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()
    24→
    25→
    26→def _model_config_dict(config: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    27→    current_model = config.get("model")
    28→    if isinstance(current_model, dict):
    29→        return dict(current_model)
    30→    if isinstance(current_model, str) and current_model.strip():
    31→        return {"default": current_model.strip()}
    32→    return {}
    33→
    34→
    35→def _set_model_provider(
    36→    config: Dict[str, Any], provider_id: str, base_url: str = ""
    37→) -> None:
    38→    model_cfg = _model_config_dict(config)
    39→    model_cfg["provider"] = provider_id
    40→    if base_url:
    41→        model_cfg["base_url"] = base_url.rstrip("/")
    42→    else:
    43→        model_cfg.pop("base_url", None)
    44→    config["model"] = model_cfg
    45→
    46→
    47→def _set_default_model(config: Dict[str, Any], model_name: str) -> None:
    48→    if not model_name:
    49→        return
    50→    model_cfg = _model_config_dict(config)
    51→    model_cfg["default"] = model_name
    52→    config["model"] = model_cfg
    53→
    54→
    55→# Default model lists per provider — used as fallback when the live
    56→# /models endpoint can't be reached.
    57→_DEFAULT_PROVIDER_MODELS = {
    58→    "zai": ["glm-5", "glm-4.7", "glm-4.5", "glm-4.5-flash"],
    59→    "kimi-coding": ["kimi-k2.5", "kimi-k2-thinking", "kimi-k2-turbo-preview"],
    60→    "minimax": ["MiniMax-M2.5", "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed", "MiniMax-M2.1"],
    61→    "minimax-cn": ["MiniMax-M2.5", "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed", "MiniMax-M2.1"],
    62→}
    63→
    64→
    65→def _setup_provider_model_selection(config, provider_id, current_model, prompt_choice, prompt_fn):
    66→    """Model selection for API-key providers with live /models detection.
    67→
    68→    Tries the provider's /models endpoint first.  Falls back to a
    69→    hardcoded default list with a warning if the endpoint is unreachable.
    70→    Always offers a 'Custom model' escape hatch.
    71→    """
    72→    from hermes_cli.auth import PROVIDER_REGISTRY
    73→    from hermes_cli.config import get_env_value
    74→    from hermes_cli.models import fetch_api_models
    75→
    76→    pconfig = PROVIDER_REGISTRY[provider_id]
    77→
    78→    # Resolve API key and base URL for the probe
    79→    api_key = ""
    80→    for ev in pconfig.api_key_env_vars:
    81→        api_key = get_env_value(ev) or os.getenv(ev, "")
    82→        if api_key:
    83→            break
    84→    base_url_env = pconfig.base_url_env_var or ""
    85→    base_url = (get_env_value(base_url_env) if base_url_env else "") or pconfig.inference_base_url
    86→
    87→    # Try live /models endpoint
    88→    live_models = fetch_api_models(api_key, base_url)
    89→
    90→    if live_models:
    91→        provider_models = live_models
    92→        print_info(f"Found {len(live_models)} model(s) from {pconfig.name} API")
    93→    else:
    94→        provider_models = _DEFAULT_PROVIDER_MODELS.get(provider_id, [])
    95→        if provider_models:
    96→            print_warning(
    97→                f"Could not auto-detect models from {pconfig.name} API — showing defaults.\n"
    98→                f"    Use \"Custom model\" if the model you expect isn't listed."
    99→            )
   100→
```

```
1→"""Hermes CLI skin/theme engine.
     2→
     3→A data-driven skin system that lets users customize the CLI's visual appearance.
     4→Skins are defined as YAML files in ~/.hermes/skins/ or as built-in presets.
     5→No code changes are needed to add a new skin.
     6→
     7→SKIN YAML SCHEMA
     8→================
     9→
    10→All fields are optional. Missing values inherit from the ``default`` skin.
    11→
    12→.. code-block:: yaml
    13→
    14→    # Required: skin identity
    15→    name: mytheme                         # Unique skin name (lowercase, hyphens ok)
    16→    description: Short description        # Shown in /skin listing
    17→
    18→    # Colors: hex values for Rich markup (banner, UI, response box)
    19→    # These are the default (dark terminal) colors.
    20→    colors:
    21→      banner_border: "#CD7F32"            # Panel border color
    22→      banner_title: "#FFD700"             # Panel title text color
    23→      banner_accent: "#FFBF00"            # Section headers (Available Tools, etc.)
    24→      banner_dim: "#B8860B"               # Dim/muted text (separators, labels)
    25→      banner_text: "#FFF8DC"              # Body text (tool names, skill names)
    26→      ui_accent: "#FFBF00"               # General UI accent
    27→      ui_label: "#4dd0e1"                # UI labels
    28→      ui_ok: "#4caf50"                   # Success indicators
    29→      ui_error: "#ef5350"                # Error indicators
    30→      ui_warn: "#ffa726"                 # Warning indicators
    31→      prompt: "#FFF8DC"                  # Prompt text color
    32→      input_rule: "#CD7F32"              # Input area horizontal rule
    33→      response_border: "#FFD700"         # Response box border (ANSI)
    34→      session_label: "#DAA520"           # Session label color
    35→      session_border: "#8B8682"          # Session ID dim color
    36→
    37→    # Light-mode color overrides (optional). Only keys that need to change.
    38→    # When theme_mode is "light", these replace the corresponding colors above.
    39→    colors_light:
    40→      banner_title: "#B8860B"
    41→      prompt: "#5C4A00"
    42→
    43→    # Spinner: customize the animated spinner during API calls
    44→    spinner:
    45→      waiting_faces:                      # Faces shown while waiting for API
    46→        - "(⚔)"
    47→        - "(⛨)"
    48→      thinking_faces:                     # Faces shown during reasoning
    49→        - "(⌁)"
    50→        - "(<>)"
    51→      thinking_verbs:                     # Verbs for spinner messages
    52→        - "forging"
    53→        - "plotting"
    54→      wings:                              # Optional left/right spinner decorations
    55→        - ["⟪⚔", "⚔⟫"]                  # Each entry is [left, right] pair
    56→        - ["⟪▲", "▲⟫"]
    57→
    58→    # Branding: text strings used throughout the CLI
    59→    branding:
    60→      agent_name: "Hermes Agent"          # Banner title, status display
    61→      welcome: "Welcome message"          # Shown at CLI startup
    62→      goodbye: "Goodbye! ⚕"              # Shown on exit
    63→      response_label: " ⚕ Hermes "       # Response box header label
    64→      prompt_symbol: "❯ "                # Input prompt symbol
    65→      help_header: "(^_^)? Commands"      # /help header text
    66→
    67→    # Tool prefix: character for tool output lines (default: ┊)
    68→    tool_prefix: "┊"
    69→
    70→USAGE
    71→=====
    72→
    73→.. code-block:: python
    74→
    75→    from hermes_cli.skin_engine import get_active_skin, list_skins, set_active_skin
    76→
    77→    skin = get_active_skin()
    78→    print(skin.colors["banner_title"])    # "#FFD700"
    79→    print(skin.get_branding("agent_name"))  # "Hermes Agent"
    80→
    81→    set_active_skin("ares")               # Switch to built-in ares skin
    82→    set_active_skin("mytheme")            # Switch to user skin from ~/.hermes/skins/
    83→
    84→BUILT-IN SKINS
    85→==============
    86→
    87→- ``default`` — Classic Hermes gold/kawaii (the current look)
    88→- ``ares``    — Crimson/bronze war-god theme with custom spinner wings
    89→- ``mono``    — Clean grayscale monochrome
    90→- ``slate``   — Cool blue developer-focused theme
    91→
    92→USER SKINS
    93→==========
    94→
    95→Drop a YAML file in ``~/.hermes/skins/<name>.yaml`` following the schema above.
    96→Activate with ``/skin <name>`` in the CLI or ``display.skin: <name>`` in config.yaml.
    97→"""
    98→
    99→import logging
   100→import os
```

```
  1106→        """
  1107→        Initialize the Hermes CLI.
  1108→
  1109→        Args:
  1110→            model: Model to use (default: from env or claude-sonnet)
  1111→            toolsets: List of toolsets to enable (default: all)
  1112→            provider: Inference provider ("auto", "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex", "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn")
  1113→            api_key: API key (default: from environment)
  1114→            base_url: API base URL (default: OpenRouter)
  1115→            max_turns: Maximum tool-calling iterations shared with subagents (default: 90)
  1116→            verbose: Enable verbose logging
  1117→            compact: Use compact display mode
  1118→            resume: Session ID to resume (restores conversation history from SQLite)
  1119→            pass_session_id: Include the session ID in the agent's system prompt
  1120→        """
  1121→        # Initialize Rich console
  1122→        self.console = Console()
  1123→        self.config = CLI_CONFIG
  1124→        self.compact = compact if compact is not None else CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("compact", False)
  1125→        # tool_progress: "off", "new", "all", "verbose" (from config.yaml display section)
  1126→        self.tool_progress_mode = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("tool_progress", "all")
  1127→        # resume_display: "full" (show history) | "minimal" (one-liner only)
  1128→        self.resume_display = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("resume_display", "full")
  1129→        # bell_on_complete: play terminal bell (\a) when agent finishes a response
  1130→        self.bell_on_complete = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("bell_on_complete", False)
  1131→        # show_reasoning: display model thinking/reasoning before the response
  1132→        self.show_reasoning = CLI_CONFIG["display"].get("show_reasoning", False)
  1133→        self.verbose = verbose if verbose is not None else (self.tool_progress_mode == "verbose")
  1134→        
  1135→        # Configuration - priority: CLI args > env vars > config file
  1136→        # Model comes from: CLI arg or config.yaml (single source of truth).
  1137→        # LLM_MODEL/OPENAI_MODEL env vars are NOT checked — config.yaml is
  1138→        # authoritative.  This avoids conflicts in multi-agent setups where
  1139→        # env vars would stomp each other.
  1140→        _model_config = CLI_CONFIG.get("model", {})
  1141→        _config_model = _model_config.get("default", "") if isinstance(_model_config, dict) else (_model_config or "")
  1142→        self.model = model or _config_model or "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6"
  1143→        # Track whether model was explicitly chosen by the user or fell back
  1144→        # to the global default.  Provider-specific normalisation may override

  1146→        self._model_is_default = not model

  1149→        self._explicit_base_url = base_url

  1152→        self.requested_provider = (
  1153→            provider
```

```
    42→def get_config_path() -> Path:
    43→    """Get the main config file path."""
    44→    return get_hermes_home() / "config.yaml"
    45→
    46→def get_env_path() -> Path:
    47→    """Get the .env file path (for API keys)."""
    48→    return get_hermes_home() / ".env"

    51→    """Get the project installation directory."""
    52→    return Path(__file__).parent.parent.resolve()

    54→def _secure_dir(path):
    55→    """Set directory to owner-only access (0700). No-op on Windows."""
    56→    try:
    57→        os.chmod(path, 0o700)
    58→    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
    59→        pass
    60→
    61→
    62→def _secure_file(path):
    63→    """Set file to owner-only read/write (0600). No-op on Windows."""
    64→    try:
    65→        if os.path.exists(str(path)):
    66→            os.chmod(path, 0o600)
    67→    except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
    68→        pass
    69→
    70→
    71→def ensure_hermes_home():
    72→    """Ensure ~/.hermes directory structure exists with secure permissions."""
    73→    home = get_hermes_home()
    74→    home.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    75→    _secure_dir(home)
    76→    for subdir in ("cron", "sessions", "logs", "memories"):
    77→        d = home / subdir
    78→        d.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    79→        _secure_dir(d)
    80→
```

Excellent! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my findings:

## Summary: Codebase Conventions

Based on my analysis of cli.py and hermes_cli/setup.py, here are the key conventions:

### 1. **Live Style/Config Updates**

**Yes, styles CAN be updated live.** Here's how:

- **Reference Storage**: The Application instance is stored in `self._app` at line 4777 (set right after creation in the `run()` method's layout setup)
- **Invalidation Method**: The `_invalidate()` method (lines 1275-1281) handles throttled repaints:
  ```python
  def _invalidate(self, min_interval: float = 0.25) -> None:
      """Throttled UI repaint — prevents terminal blinking on slow/SSH connections."""
      if hasattr(self, "_app") and self._app and (now - self._last_invalidate) >= min_interval:
          self._last_invalidate = now
          self._app.invalidate()
  ```
- **Pattern Used**: Throughout the codebase, `app.invalidate()` and `event.app.invalidate()` are called to refresh the UI (e.g., lines 3949, 3957, 3974, 4051, 4067, etc.)
- **For `/verbose` and `/reasoning`**: These commands don't trigger visual updates—they just modify state flags (`self.verbose`, `self.show_reasoning`). The visual update happens only when the next message arrives and the UI re-renders.

### 2. **Setup Section Organization**

**Display settings currently live in the "Agent Settings" section** (lines 1645-1844 in setup.py):

The `SETUP_SECTIONS` tuple at line 2308 defines the structure:
```python
SETUP_SECTIONS = [
    ("model", "Model & Provider", setup_model_provider),
    ("terminal", "Terminal Backend", setup_terminal_backend),
    ("gateway", "Messaging Platforms (Gateway)", setup_gateway),
    ("tools", "Tools", setup_tools),
    ("agent", "Agent Settings", setup_agent_settings),
]
```

**Within "Agent Settings", display settings are mixed with behavior settings:**
- **Lines 1674-1692**: Tool progress mode (`tool_progress`)
- **Lines 1694-1723**: Terminal theme mode (`theme_mode`)
- **Lines 1725-1745**: Context compression settings
- **Lines 1747-1819**: Session reset policy

There is **NO dedicated "Display" section**. Display settings are currently scattered within the "Agent Settings" section, alongside max iterations, compression, and session reset config. The section uses `config["display"]` as a dictionary to store: `tool_progress`, `theme_mode`, `show_reasoning`, `bell_on_complete`, `resume_display`, and `compact`.

### 3. **Background Detection at Startup**

**Yes, there is precedent for background threads during startup.** The pattern is established in the `run()` method (lines 4779-4865):

```python
# Spinner loop (daemon thread) - line 4789
spinner_thread = threading.Thread(target=spinner_loop, daemon=True)
spinner_thread.start()

# Process loop (daemon thread) - line 4864
process_thread = threading.Thread(target=process_loop, daemon=True)
process_thread.start()
```

**Background task execution** is also already supported via `_run_background_task()` (lines 3060-3080):
```python
# Background task tracking: {task_id: threading.Thread}
self._background_tasks: Dict[str, threading.Thread] = {}

thread = threading.Thread(target=run_background, daemon=True, name=f"bg-task-{task_id}")
self._background_tasks[task_id] = thread
thread.start()
```

This pattern shows the codebase is comfortable with:
- Creating daemon threads during app initialization
- Storing references for invalidation tracking
- Using `threading.Thread` with `daemon=True`
- Storing thread refs in instance dicts for tracking and cleanup

```
1→"""Clipboard image extraction for macOS, Linux, and WSL2.
     2→
     3→Provides a single function `save_clipboard_image(dest)` that checks the
     4→system clipboard for image data, saves it to *dest* as PNG, and returns
     5→True on success.  No external Python dependencies — uses only OS-level
     6→CLI tools that ship with the platform (or are commonly installed).
     7→
     8→Platform support:
     9→  macOS  — osascript (always available), pngpaste (if installed)
    10→  WSL2   — powershell.exe via .NET System.Windows.Forms.Clipboard
    11→  Linux  — wl-paste (Wayland), xclip (X11)
    12→"""
    13→
    14→import base64
    15→import logging
    16→import os
    17→import subprocess
    18→import sys
    19→from pathlib import Path
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→# Cache WSL detection (checked once per process)
    24→_wsl_detected: bool | None = None
    25→
    26→
    27→def save_clipboard_image(dest: Path) -> bool:
    28→    """Extract an image from the system clipboard and save it as PNG.
    29→
    30→    Returns True if an image was found and saved, False otherwise.
    31→    """
    32→    dest.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    33→    if sys.platform == "darwin":
    34→        return _macos_save(dest)
    35→    return _linux_save(dest)
    36→
    37→
    38→def has_clipboard_image() -> bool:
    39→    """Quick check: does the clipboard currently contain an image?
    40→
    41→    Lighter than save_clipboard_image — doesn't extract or write anything.
    42→    """
    43→    if sys.platform == "darwin":
    44→        return _macos_has_image()
    45→    if _is_wsl():
    46→        return _wsl_has_image()
    47→    if os.environ.get("WAYLAND_DISPLAY"):
    48→        return _wayland_has_image()
    49→    return _xclip_has_image()
    50→
    51→
    52→# ── macOS ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
    53→
    54→def _macos_save(dest: Path) -> bool:
    55→    """Try pngpaste first (fast, handles more formats), fall back to osascript."""
    56→    return _macos_pngpaste(dest) or _macos_osascript(dest)
    57→
    58→
    59→def _macos_has_image() -> bool:
    60→    """Check if macOS clipboard contains image data."""
```

```
1024→def save_config_value(key_path: str, value: any) -> bool:
  1025→    """
  1026→    Save a value to the active config file at the specified key path.
  1027→    
  1028→    Respects the same lookup order as load_cli_config():
  1029→    1. ~/.hermes/config.yaml (user config - preferred, used if it exists)
  1030→    2. ./cli-config.yaml (project config - fallback)
  1031→    
  1032→    Args:
  1033→        key_path: Dot-separated path like "agent.system_prompt"
  1034→        value: Value to save
  1035→    
  1036→    Returns:
  1037→        True if successful, False otherwise
  1038→    """
  1039→    # Use the same precedence as load_cli_config: user config first, then project config
  1040→    user_config_path = Path.home() / '.hermes' / 'config.yaml'
  1041→    project_config_path = Path(__file__).parent / 'cli-config.yaml'
  1042→    config_path = user_config_path if user_config_path.exists() else project_config_path
  1043→    
  1044→    try:
  1045→        # Ensure parent directory exists (for ~/.hermes/config.yaml on first use)
  1046→        config_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  1047→        
  1048→        # Load existing config
  1049→        if config_path.exists():
  1050→            with open(config_path, 'r') as f:
  1051→                config = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
  1052→        else:
  1053→            config = {}
  1054→        
  1055→        # Navigate to the key and set value
  1056→        keys = key_path.split('.')
  1057→        current = config
  1058→        for key in keys[:-1]:
  1059→            if key not in current or not isinstance(current[key], dict):
  1060→                current[key] = {}
  1061→            current = current[key]
  1062→        current[keys[-1]] = value
  1063→        
  1064→        # Save back
  1065→        with open(config_path, 'w') as f:
  1066→            yaml.dump(config, f, default_flow_style=False, sort_keys=False)
  1067→        
  1068→        # Enforce owner-only permissions on config files (contain API keys)
  1069→        try:
  1070→            os.chmod(config_path, 0o600)
  1071→        except (OSError, NotImplementedError):
  1072→            pass
  1073→        
  1074→        return True
  1075→    except Exception as e:
  1076→        logger.error("Failed to save config: %s", e)
  1077→        return False
  1078→
  1079→
  1080→# ============================================================================
  1081→# HermesCLI Class
  1082→# ============================================================================
  1083→
```

```
  3155→            self.agent.verbose_logging = self.verbose
  3156→            self.agent.quiet_mode = not self.verbose
```

```
  3227→    def _on_reasoning(self, reasoning_text: str):
  3228→        """Callback for intermediate reasoning display during tool-call loops."""
```

## Summary of Codebase Conventions

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here are the three conventions:

### 1. **Command Argument Handling & Conflict Resolution**

The codebase uses a **simple string-based dispatch pattern for slash commands with no subcommand infrastructure**. Here's how it works:

**Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 2800-2925)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py` (lines 2242+)

**Key Pattern:**
- **Slash commands with arguments** use manual string parsing, not subcommand subparsers
  - `/model` → checks `cmd_lower.startswith("/model")`, splits on whitespace: `cmd_original.split(maxsplit=1)`
  - `/skin` → same pattern: splits to extract argument, checks for special keywords ("light", "dark", "auto")
  - `/reasoning` → checks for predefined values ("show", "hide", "none", "low", "medium", "high", "xhigh")
  - `/prompt` → parses into new prompt text
  - `/personality` → looks up in a dict of personality names

**Conflict Handling:**
- **Order matters**: Commands are checked in a specific order with `elif cmd_lower.startswith()` chains (line 2800+)
- **Case sensitivity**: Commands are lowercased for comparison (`cmd_lower`), but original case is preserved where needed (`cmd_original`) to maintain user input like model names "Anthropic/Claude-Opus-4"
- **Validation**: Arguments are validated against allowed values before applying
  - Example: `/model` validates model exists in provider's catalog before switching
  - Example: `/skin` validates skin name exists in available skins list (line 3131-3135)
  - Example: `/personality` checks if name is in `self.personalities` dict (line 2426)

**No subcommand infrastructure**: The CLI's `hermes_cli/main.py` uses argparse subcommands for **terminal commands** (e.g., `hermes model`, `hermes gateway run`) but **slash commands in the agent** deliberately avoid this pattern for UX simplicity. The agent slash commands are single-level: `/command [argument]`.

---

### 2. **Cached/Persisted Detection Results**

The pattern is **lazy detection with in-memory caching, persisted to disk only when explicitly changed**. The best example is theme mode detection:

**Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/skin_engine.py` (lines 578-750)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/colors.py` (lines 26-50)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 1024-1077)

**Key Pattern:**
- **Module-level globals cache the detected value:**
  ```python
  _theme_mode: str = "auto"  # User's config setting
  _resolved_theme_mode: Optional[str] = None  # Cached auto-detection result
  ```

- **Detection happens on first access:**
  ```python
  def get_theme_mode() -> str:
      global _resolved_theme_mode
      if _theme_mode in ("light", "dark"):
          return _theme_mode
      # Auto-detect on first call
      if _resolved_theme_mode is None:
          result = detect_terminal_background()  # OSC 11 escape sequence
          _resolved_theme_mode = result if result != "unknown" else "dark"
      return _resolved_theme_mode
  ```

- **Cache is invalidated when user changes setting:**
  ```python
  def set_theme_mode(mode: str) -> None:
      global _theme_mode, _resolved_theme_mode
      _theme_mode = mode
      _resolved_theme_mode = None  # Reset cache so auto re-detects
  ```

- **Persistence: config.yaml in ~/.hermes/**
  - When user runs `/skin dark`, value is saved via: `save_config_value("display.theme_mode", arg)`
  - Uses `~/.hermes/config.yaml` as the primary source of truth
  - Falls back to `./cli-config.yaml` if user config doesn't exist

**No separate .detected cache files:** The codebase doesn't use separate detection cache files. Detection happens in-process and persisted to the main config. For clipboard detection, a similar pattern exists with `_wsl_detected` module-level cache (line 24 of clipboard.py).

---

### 3. **Platform-Specific Code (Windows vs Unix)**

The codebase uses **simple platform guards with helper functions**:

**Files:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/gateway.py` (lines 108-115)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/config.py` (line 26)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/clipboard.py` (lines 24-49)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/hermes_cli/main.py` (scattered throughout)

**Key Pattern:**

**Option A: Helper functions (preferred in gateway.py)**
```python
def is_linux() -> bool:
    return sys.platform.startswith('linux')

def is_macos() -> bool:
    return sys.platform == 'darwin'

def is_windows() -> bool:
    return sys.platform == 'win32'
```

**Option B: Direct platform checks with platform.system() (used in config.py, setup.py)**
```python
import platform
_IS_WINDOWS = platform.system() == "Windows"
_IS_LINUX = platform.system() == "Linux"
_IS_MACOS = platform.system() == "Darwin"
```
These are set at module load time and reused throughout.

**Option C: Inline sys.platform checks (used in main.py, clipboard.py)**
```python
if sys.platform == "darwin":
    return _macos_save(dest)
return _linux_save(dest)

if sys.platform == "win32":
    venv_pip = PROJECT_ROOT / "venv" / "Scripts" / "pip"
else:
    venv_pip = PROJECT_ROOT / "venv" / "bin" / "pip"
```

**Convention Summary:**
- **Prefer `sys.platform` over `os.name`** (os.name check not found in codebase)
- **Use `platform.system()` for clean display names** when showing to users
- **Set module-level boolean flags for constants** that are checked multiple times
- **Use inline platform checks for one-off conditions**
- **WSL detection uses `_is_wsl()` helper function** (clipboard.py)
- **File paths adapt**: Windows uses `Scripts\pip`, Unix uses `bin/pip`
- **Permission setting is platform-aware**: `os.chmod()` wrapped in try/except since Windows doesn't support Unix permissions

**No centralized platform module:** Each module handles its own platform checks; there's no single "platform detection" module that all others depend on.

---
